## November 18, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Full magical effects are twice Mixed at equal slot count (LATEST)

**Author approved the tested simple 2× Full-vs-Mixed effect relationship** rather than granting Full one extra slot. For **the same invested slots 1–6**, **Mixed** retains a smaller continuous attribute enhancement plus normal eligible magical effect; **Full** has **no bonus attributes** but produces **twice Mixed's eligible numerical magical effect**. Blank remains attribute-only. The historical Full-one-effect-tier-above-Mixed model, and any suggestion of +1 Full slot or magical strength scaling with host item Quality, are **superseded**. Full doubles **only the specified effect budget**, not ordinary weapon/armor Power, item Quality, number of attacks or number of enchantment slots.

**Operative elemental examples:** Mixed common delivered elemental Power by slots 1–6 = **430/1,350/3,800/5,800/8,100/15,000**; Full = **860/2,700/7,600/11,600/16,200/30,000** per lawful contact. An eligible conditional Attack Power bonus such as *hypothetical* Mantis +100% would become +200% in Full, not twice total damage. **Not approved by this decision:** Mantis's actual six slot bonus values, Full/Mixed-specific Mantis win rates, a new attribute-per-slot table, typed SR formula, non-numeric effects, activation costs, socket access, or more MP capacity. The previous controlled tests suggested near parity across three elemental families but used provisional Mixed stat allocations and typed SR; do not present their numerical win rates as canon. Retain existing elements' independently approved delivery, status and Power-only-rider scopes.

**Updated files:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. All routine documentation commits directly to `main`.

## November 18, 2026 — Hypothetical Sharpness enchantment power benchmark AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST)

**APPROVED DESIGN BENCHMARK ONLY, NOT AN AVAILABLE ENCHANTMENT OR NEW DAMAGE RULE:** For comparing proposed enchantments and future spells, use an always-active hypothetical Sharpness effect of **+5% final physical Attack Power per invested enchantment slot**, with 1–6 slots = **+5% / +10% / +15% / +20% / +25% / +30% total**. Apply once to completed physical Attack Power, not to STR/DEX or F before squaring. Example at five slots: `Power_sharpness = 1.25 × Power_mundane`. This does not establish any actual spell damage/MP/SP price or remove conditional activation and action-economy tradeoffs.

**Precision and precedence:** The author selected a simple approximate comparison ruler, **not** guaranteed 50/50 win rates at every slot. Earlier provisional element-only win-rate modeling suggested nearer **+40% at six slots** than +30%; that difference is retained as an uncertainty to retest, not a revision of the approved simplified benchmark. Previous studies used a NOT-APPROVED typed Status Resistance model `(effective END + 0.65 × MR)^2`, so actual parity may change. Enchantment strength depends on its **1–6 occupied slots** rather than host equipment Quality. Mundane physical Quality still affects normal weapon and armor calculations separately. Hypothetical Sharpness is **never a purchasable enchantment**; Mantis Blade percentage progression and Blank/Mixed/Full-to-slot resource remapping remain unresolved. Existing approved elemental weapon per-contact Power 430/1,350/3,800/5,800/8,100/15,000 and elemental statuses are unchanged.

## November 18, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: enchantments scale by invested slots, not Quality (LATEST)

**Author's latest explicit correction:** Weapon enchantments do not have separate Quality, and the host equipment's mundane Quality does not further modify their effect strength. Enchantment output is governed by **1–6 occupied slots**. This applies to elemental weapon enchantments, including the fire edge family (Burning/Ember Edge), and to the in-development Mantis Blade / Mantis Strike. **Approved unified elemental Power per lawful contact, by slots 1–6: 430 / 1,350 / 3,800 / 5,800 / 8,100 / 15,000.** Supersedes older Quality I–V Mixed and Quality V Full one-higher effect-tier descriptions **as rules for enchantment potency**, while preserving mundane gear Quality's independent physical benefits and previously approved elemental status effects.

**Still OPEN:** How the former Blank/Mixed/Full arrangements, item slot eligibility, Leywell capacities, mana costs and Tier VI access map onto the slot-only model. No new resource formula is inferred. **Mantis bonus percentages remain unapproved test candidates** (the +75%-per-slot starting curve and the subsequent slot-specific win-rate calibration are not canon). ## November 18, 2026 — Author approves stronger unified elemental Power I–VI (LATEST)

**Author approval in conversation:** The previously proposed stronger elemental weapon Power numbers are **approved**. ## November 18, 2026 — Uniform elemental tiers, Lightning discharge gate and typed SR (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTIONS)

**Author decisions:** Reject assistant's proposed lesser Tier I–V Power multipliers for Ice/Lightning/Air/Hex and **give every elemental weapon the same Power at the same effect tier as Fire**. Preserve access to effect **Tier VI** on eligible Quality V Full enchanted equipment and approved Tier VI Leywell; the numerical Tier VI magnitude is still open. **Lightning discharge below 1× effective Lightning Status Resistance does nothing**; build Static remains inert until a valid discharge. The user's earlier Ice mechanics (1×: SPD −15% and half movement tiles; 4×: Frozen, no ordinary actions but magic or STR² per-turn struggle; 12×: Entombed with no self-escape), Lightning discharge mechanics (1×: three-round Shocked at −1 action/turn and negation of a current action; 4×: one-round Stunned and Trapped with Shocked), and Hex mechanics (personal and equipped Leywell MP drain, injury if personal MP exhausted, 4× ends ongoing magic and drains more) are recorded in `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`. Water MR erosion, Acid AR erosion and Air fanning/explosion remain unchanged.

Explicit balancing goal: common stronger elemental Power versus MR0, somewhat worse performance versus peer Glassweave, and strongly poor performance versus high MR Nullporcelain. **NOT APPROVED numerical tests:** `SR_element=(END+0.65MR)^2×R_element`, and common tier I–VI Power `430/1350/3800/5800/8100/15000`. ## November 18, 2026 — Water erosion and Air fanning/detonation direction (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

The author explicitly distinguishes **Water** and **Air** from the universal half-SR self-PP default. **Water** accumulates magnitude to **reduce Magic Resistance**, a counterpart to Acid's approved Armor Rating erosion. **Air** at **0.5× SR** adds **half its magnitude to other elemental effects**; **above 1× SR** other elements' magnitudes are **doubled for their final effects**; at **4× SR** all accumulated elemental magnitude is **expended at quadruple power in a damaging explosive attack** and all elemental magnitude conditions end. Preserve the existing legal delivery, END² Status Resistance and three-turn accumulation/refresh system. Quantitative MR loss, air contribution/multiplier sequence, blast damage conversion/area and how ×4 applies to multiple magnitude pools are unresolved, not silently approved. This supersedes the previous blanket one self-Pressure Point assignment for Water and Air. Fire's approved injury cadence and other elements' still-proposed higher-band outcomes remain unchanged. Owners: `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md` and `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`.

## November 18, 2026 — Extend Burning's accumulation engine to other elemental statuses (AUTHOR DIRECTIVE; OUTCOMES PENDING REVIEW)

The author requests a mirror of the calibrated Burning status structure for established elemental types. The **shared engine** now applies to eligible ice/cold, lightning/shock, water, air, toxic/hex, and acid/corrosion: actual eligible delivered elemental Power accumulates separately by type; the three-turn condition **refreshes on each valid same-element reapplication**; the **0.5× / >1× / 4× END-derived Status Resistance bands** designate trace, one continuing self-PP, substantial elemental effect, and intensified effect. Keep shields, real media, specific resistances, ordinary protections, cleansing and environmental changes. **Element-specific threshold outcomes are not newly approved** by this mirror: Fire's burning damage/doubling does not impose damage on water, air, hex, etc. Earlier lightning stun, water/air displacement and elemental-resistance debuff tables remain historical proposals. Approved acid AR reduction (floor 0) and conditional lightning conduction remain in effect without invented numbers. Owning detailed review docket: `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`; equipment cross-reference: `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`. Unresolved: individual outcomes, names, exact SR equation, non-fire per-tier Power, tick/expiry rules, cleanup and environmental modifiers.

## November 18, 2026 — Status Resistance, Burning Edge Power and fire injuries APPROVED

**Status Resistance (SR) = (effective END)²**, including the full value of eligible equipment END and the usual effective-attribute modifiers. **Burning Edge I–V** delivers **226 / 554 / 1,335 / 2,456 / 3,914** Fire Power per eligible contact. A contact adds Fire Magnitude and refreshes the three-turn fire duration while preserving the rounds already spent Burning. Extinguishing clears the effect.

**Damage cadence:** Above SR, Burning inflicts **one injury stage every second round spent Burning**. From four times SR, Engulfed inflicts **two stages each round**. Use ordinary injury and Shimmer Guard progression, with no second Impact-to-Defense comparison for a fire tick. From half SR the status imposes one ongoing self-Pressure Point in total. This supersedes the experimental burn-Power conversion and earlier Tier I–V ladder.

**References updated:** Canon Index, World and Shimmer, Equipment and Economy, Combat and Shimmer Guard, Modular Equipment draft, and Magical Media and Contests. The [calibration notes](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md) record the test assumptions and results for generalists and builds investing up to half their discretionary attribute points in END. **Open:** Tier VI Fire Power, exact within-turn timing, specific environmental and cleansing values, and refresh for other elements.

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge renews active fire on contact (AUTHOR APPROVED)

An eligible Burning Edge hit adds its delivered Fire Power to Fire Magnitude and refreshes the target’s three-turn fire condition, including while Smoldering, Burning or Engulfed. Extinguishing still clears the condition. The exact within-turn expiry boundary and refresh behavior of other elements remain open. The approved cadence is recorded above.

**Balance direction:** Test against generalists and builds allocating up to 50% of discretionary level-up attribute points to END. More extreme END specialists are outside the balance targets. The proposed 25% weighting for equipment END was rejected. The approved Status Resistance equation, Tier I–V Fire Power and injury cadence are recorded above.

**References updated:** Canon Index, World and Shimmer, Equipment and Economy, Combat and Shimmer Guard, Modular Equipment draft, and Magical Media and Contests.

## November 18, 2026 — New accumulated Elemental Magnitude and END-derived Status Resistance (FRAMEWORK APPROVAL)

**Author's redesign:** Elemental Magnitude is now the amount of **elemental Power that actually contacts and reaches a target**, accumulating in a **three-turn elemental condition** each time that kind of elemental attack makes legal contact. New universal **Status Resistance scales with END**. The approved equation is in the later entry above. Fire: under **0.5 SR** smoldering does nothing; **at least 0.5 SR** one meaningful self-PP; **above SR** ongoing burning damage; **at least 4 SR** twice normal burning damage. Mundane and magical acts can cleanse or intensify this condition, including water/oil and conductive metal with lightning. Earlier SPD ignition, separate END severity test, perpetual burn-until-put-out as an uncapped default, Smoldering inherent Fire Resistance decrease, and generic Contact Factor are superseded. Preserve genuine contact, existing medium, armor and shield rules, five-round Leywell enchantment activation, tier VI Mana Blade, two-action economy and peer-level parity targets.

**Historical calibration:** The former Fire Power proposal was 204/421/955/1705/2671/6678 for I–VI. The new Tier I–V values supersede the first five entries. The last value remains a Tier VI proposal. See the [Modular Equipment draft](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Remove generic Contact Factor and record historical SPD ignition and END intensity

**Approved corrections:** **Discard Contact Factor completely as a generic elemental calculation**; do not multiply elemental Power/Magnitude by the old fire-metal ×1.25, insulation ×0.5 or any other global delivery multiplier. Genuine heat/energy pathways, struck shields/barriers, material properties, insulation and separately established specific Elemental Resistance still apply normally and should not be double counted. **Lightning** may receive its **own bonus against real conductive metal armor** where the shock actually reaches the wearer, but the bonus value/conditions remain for separate design.

**Burning Edge uses distinct defensive jobs:** On legal elemental contact **SPD determines chance to ignite**; once ignition succeeds, **END and appropriate Fire Resistance determine fire intensity and injury severity**, not SPD. A burning effect then persists until genuinely extinguished or deprived of fuel, **without repeated SPD/END checks to set its duration or intensity**. Slower targets ignite more easily, but their burn duration is not currently determined by an automatic SPD timer. **Smoldering** is the fallback when the target ignites but resists fire injury, delivering one meaningful self-PP and a modest fire-only Resistance reduction; the exact percent is still OPEN. Keep tiers I–V peer-relative effectiveness, the Tier VI Mana Blade exception, real media and approved Leywell costs unchanged. Old numerical Flaming Edge/Contact Factor proposals remain historical and unapproved. Owners: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Modular Draft](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge correction: initial Speed ignition, persistent flame and Smoldering (LATEST)

**Latest author correction overrides earlier November 18 SPD-afterburn interpretation:** SPD affects **whether an eligible weapon's fire ignites the target**, NOT the duration of an already-lit fire. After ignition no per-round SPD or END contest determines the fire's lifespan: it burns until real extinguishing/fuel loss. Initial fire damage and protection are resolved separately and once at the appropriate contact/ignition event. **Smoldering is meaningful:** where the fire injury/damage is resisted but smoldering takes hold, it imposes a **modest reduction to specific Fire Resistance** and **one self-applied meaningful Pressure Point** while active, not automatic injury. Exact resistance reduction (assistant suggested −10%), precise conditions and duration beyond physical extinguishing are OPEN.

**Balance principle:** Increasing Burning Edge I–V tiers should preserve **similar relative injury and status consequences against peer-level, similarly equipped Heroes** instead of automatically escalating status severity as both sides level; higher tiers remain more dangerous to comparatively weaker targets. Tier VI Mana Blade is the agreed limited-ammo peak exception. Existing real-contact model, media, MR/specific fire resistance and legal damage/action economy remain unchanged. Previous assistant's tier-I–V auto-severity ladder, duration vs SPD, Fire Magnitude **24/34/51/69/86/120**, and ignition odds are unapproved experiments. Old *Contact Factor* multipliers were unapproved; the assistant has suggested replacing a universal factor with actual pathway and specific protection, still awaiting author decision. Owning [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and [Draft](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge name and ongoing-fire Speed defense (AUTHOR APPROVED; exact numbers OPEN)

**Approved:** Fire elemental weapon enchantments I–V share the simple name **Burning Edge**; the prior suggested separate rank names are not used. The wholly fiery **Mana Blade** is Tier VI, disappears when not powered, and is distinct from a physical steel blade plus a free independent full attack. Ordinary Burning Edge applies a **limited, genuine-contact Elemental Magnitude rider** rather than multiplying weapon/character physical Power. The **continuing burn after ignition is resisted by Speed (effective SPD), not END**. Initial ignition separately respects Elemental Magnitude, legitimate initial Fire Resistance including END where appropriate, contact pathways and physical insulation. Exact numerical Fire Magnitude, status progression, afterburn durations, action effects and injury resolution are still **PROPOSALS ONLY**. Do not promote unapproved Candidate C additive Weapon Rating numbers or the prior draft Rank V whole-element transformation. ## November 18, 2026 — Tier VI Leywell, fire Mana Blade and equip-time approval (LATEST)

**Approved:** Full enchantments carry a Leywell **one tier above** the equivalent Mixed item, extending the 10-MP-per-tier capacity ladder to **Tier VI 60 MP**; prior statements that Tier V 50 MP is the maximum for all gear are superseded. **Approved Tier VI activation costs: Minor 4 / Standard 8 / Powerful 24 MP.** The author agrees to Tier VI **Mana Blade**, whose **entire blade consists of fire and vanishes when unpowered**. Move the older wholly elemental Rank V weapon concept to Tier VI. Ordinary lower-tier fire enchantments continue as real-medium-dependent conditional elemental contact riders, with draft coefficient/Elemental Magnitude/resistance numbers still UNAPPROVED. A Powerful Mana Blade's five-round activation costs 24 MP, allowing **2 activations** from its full 60-MP Tier VI Leywell.

**Item handling approved:** Removing a worn ring and putting on a replacement, or a comparable small accessory exchange, is **one combat action total**. Donning Light armor takes **2 actions**, Medium armor **2 minutes**, Heavy armor **10 minutes**. Doffing and fitting interruptions remain undecided. Preserve ordinary interaction, weapon draw/throw, two active rings and one offensive action per turn. **No change to the not-yet-canon additive Weapon Rating formula, or numerical flame injury magnitude**. ## November 18, 2026 — Enchantment resource identities and item interactions (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Enchantment modes ratified:** Blank = continuous full stat boost paid through Soul Strain, Mixed = continuous smaller stat boost paid through Soul Strain **plus** Leywell-powered effect with **no extra Soul Strain**, Full = **no stat boost** and a Leywell-powered magical effect **one effect tier higher**, with no active-effect Soul Strain. Existing Quality I–V stat reference values are retained. Full effects use the **same normal MP cost as their actual higher tier**, not an additional multiplier. **Tier VI effect** at QV Full is conceptually approved: **one simple magical effect with base output on par with a moderate specialist operating at their peak**, but very few activations from a normal maximum **Tier V 50-MP Leywell**. Do not grant the specialist's complete Craft, new item Quality or Leywell Tier VI. Exact VI effect/MP numbers remain unresolved. Continuous stat bonuses do **not** require five-round MP reactivation; activated effects continue to use Leywells and their own timing. [Owning equipment entry](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md).

**General interaction rules approved:** Retrieving accessible items or interacting with the physical world (e.g. opening a door) costs **one combat action**. One action can draw or sheathe **up to two weapons**. Exception: with a free hand or a hand freed from a two-handed weapon, **one readied thrown weapon carried in a bandolier/quiver may be drawn and thrown in one action**, counting against the existing one offensive attack-action limit. Swapping equipped rings or other gear requires actual interaction actions; a carried cache of offensive magical rings cannot be instantly cycled for free and does not increase the approved two active ring positions. Exact full-slot ring remove/replace action count remains unresolved, as do unapproved individual Tier VI activation costs. [Combat reference](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Three enchantment modes and prospective Tier VI effect (AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Latest author direction:** Establish **Blank** (stats only), **Mixed** (moderate stats + normal magical effect), and **Full** (no stats + distinctly stronger effect) as the three equipment-enchantment modes. Keep historic Quality I–V stat benchmarks: Pure **+6/+12/+20/+28/+36** as the Blank reference, Mixed **+3/+6/+10/+14/+18** as the Mixed reference. The author favors **Full magical effects one tier above Mixed**, mapping QI–V items to effect tiers II–VI instead of I–V. The sixth is a **potential Full-only enchantment effect tier**, not a new item Quality or Leywell tier; exact Tier VI effects/activation MP and numeric scale are NOT yet approved. The quality-versus-effect distinction, existing Leywell maximum 50 MP and existing combat/medium rules are preserved. Owning section: [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md).

**Still open:** Detail mode distribution and installation restrictions; continuous vs five-round Blank stat enhancement upkeep; actual effect/MP schedules, especially Tier VI; stacking and design balance.

## November 18, 2026 — Secretly prepared active enchanted defense (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Active defensive enchantments follow the existing prepared defensive-action model (Parry and applicable prepared magical defenses), not an on-demand free reaction.** On the defender's own turn, **secretly commit the required one or two combat actions**, identify the specific equipped defensive enchantment, and declare a **narrow, legal, outcome-independent trigger** in advance. The prepared enchantment takes effect at its lawful response opportunity if that trigger occurs and all requirements (including appropriate Leywell power, physical medium where relevant, and ordinary positioning/eligibility) hold. The defender cannot wait to see whether the attack would hit or deal damage before choosing the trigger or defense.

**Examples:** “The next attack against this named ally” or “the next attack made by this specified enemy” may qualify when the item actually supports that coverage. “The next attack that hits me” is not eligible as an outcome-dependent trigger. Follow the already approved preparation/expiry and relevant action limitations of the defensive framework being used; no additional defensive action, free attack, or reserved action beyond the existing two-action economy is created. Magical defense mechanics are not automatically granted the Parry-specific +1/+2 hit category, Pressure Point rewards, cooldown, or shield routing unless the individual enchantment explicitly earns such behavior under its actual approved rules.

**Distinction preserved:** Action-free passive modifications of an existing defense (e.g., an eligible preactivated reinforcement effect) remain passive; an enchantment that **independently performs an active defensive maneuver** consumes the preparatory action(s) and obeys the secret-condition system. The five-round duration of ordinary magical enhancements is distinct from the time window of a prepared, triggered active defense. Further item-specific duration on activation, expenditure timing if a trigger never occurs, trigger expiry, response scheduling, multiple prepared effects and coordination with the single prepared spell constraint require explicit calibration. They are **not** authorization for unlimited simultaneous active defensive reactions.

## November 18, 2026 — Passive versus active enchantment action economy (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Classification by behavior, not simply by whether magical energy is spent:** An enchantment that merely **modifies a legal action or enables a new capability** is a *passive/modifying activation* and **costs zero combat actions to switch on**, even when it consumes Leywell MP and maintains a time-limited magical effect. Examples include igniting a sword, boosting movement speed, or enabling an alternate mode of movement without actually moving the user. A character may activate **as many eligible passive/modifying enchantments as desired during their own turn**, subject to actual installed items, sufficient correctly colored Leywell mana, necessary real media, ordinary eligibility and any separately approved restrictions; no universal one-passive-per-turn cap.

**Independent actions:** An enchanted item that **actually performs an action the user would normally spend actions on**, including moving the wielder, launching its own attack, or actively defending, requires **one or two combat actions** according to the effect's design. These active uses must respect the existing **two-action turn and maximum one offensive attack action**; a magically produced attack is not an extra free strike merely because an item creates it. The exact 1-versus-2 action assignment remains for individual enchantments.

**Durations:** Previously approved ordinary enchantment activation lasts **five combat rounds** unless an explicit item rule provides an exception. Action-free activation does **not** mean MP-free activation. Independent actions and sustained modifying effects still consume their relevant Leywell MP according to applicable costs.

**Timing and interruption questions OPEN:** Resolve whether an **ordinary spell in the act of casting or channeling can be interrupted by damage**, and if so which damage events qualify, whether a check is required, what happens to paid MP and partially completed actions, and which separately approved exceptions survive. **Use that spellcasting decision as a reference before deciding whether damage similarly interrupts the existing two-action Leywell-recharge operation.** Do not assume all damage automatically cancels charging, or that it can never interrupt charging. Active enchanted defenses also require future integration with prepared defensive spell timing, turn windows and existing reaction/action constraints. None of these unresolved questions alters existing specific Shocked or anti-magic interruption effects.

## November 18, 2026 — Leywell charging action, rounding, and five-round enchantment activation (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Transfer accounting:** A legitimate contributor spends their own MP of the Leywell's exact matching color. Apply the approved **90% transfer efficiency** and **round the amount actually deposited DOWN to whole MP** after calculating the transferred amount; never round each constituent point up. Thus 10 MP spent → 9 stored, while 5 MP spent → 4 stored. Charging cannot exceed the Leywell's maximum (Tier I–V capacities **10/20/30/40/50 MP**) or the contributor's available MP. Fractional transfer yield is lost, not retained as partial MP. The contributor bears the actual spent MP cost, even when the destination fills before all theoretical yield would fit; efficient contributors may choose the amount transferred.

**In-combat recharge timing:** Charging a Leywell takes **two combat actions (approximately six seconds of real-world time)**. This is an action cost for a charging operation, not two additional mana charges or an automatic mana-generation process. The amount actually chargeable in one operation is bounded by available compatible MP, 90% efficiency rounded down, and remaining Leywell capacity; no additional fixed per-action transfer cap is approved here. The physical access, interruption and out-of-combat procedural details remain open.

**Ordinary enchantment activation:** **Most enchanted-item effects activate without spending a combat action**, and each normal activation lasts **five combat rounds**. The effect uses its Leywell for its applicable activation MP cost. An enchantment's explicit exception can have different timing only where separately approved; activation is not a free independent offensive attack or a license to exceed established action/effect limitations. Do not infer unlimited stacking or a free extra strike. Whether repeated activation refreshes the duration, and exact simultaneous activation limits, remain open.

**Approved working enchantment activation-cost ladder (user accepted preceding proposal):**

| Leywell tier | Minor effect MP | Standard effect MP | Powerful effect MP |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| I | 1 | 2 | 4 |
| II | 1 | 3 | 5 |
| III | 2 | 4 | 7 |
| IV | 2 | 5 | 10 |
| V | 3 | 6 | 12 |

These are general **initial design bands**, not an automatic cost or power assignment to every effect; individual enchantments still need compatible color, accessible medium where relevant, and actual effect design/testing. Previous assistant-suggested ten-round duration is superseded by five rounds. Existing exceptional material equipment effects (e.g. Skyvein's approved 6 MP per powered turn) remain under their owning specific rules unless expressly revised.

## November 18, 2026 — Leywell direct-MP recharge, fixed efficiency and five-tier capacity (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Latest author correction supersedes earlier devotional recharge:** Charging a Leywell **spends the contributing character's own actual MP**. It is **not** an independent mana-generating prayer process, does not create free MP, and does not depend on sincerity, faith, or devotional enthusiasm. Transfer efficiency is a flat **90% for every contributor**, independent of belief: a transfer of 10 MP from a legal source deposits 9 MP, with 1 MP lost. The precise accounting of fractional MP deposits and partial transfers remains open, so no further rounding rule is inferred.

**Strict color identity:** Each Leywell is bound to **one mana color**. It **only accepts transferred mana of exactly that matching color**; e.g., a blue Leywell cannot accept red mana. It does not transmute colors. The contributor must actually have spendable mana of the required color; an affinity alone is not free mana.

**Approved Leywell capacity by tier:** Tier **I = 10 MP, II = 20 MP, III = 30 MP, IV = 40 MP, V = 50 MP**, maximum stored amount. These are Leywell tiers, not automatic character-level grants. No Leywell may be charged above capacity. The former proposed 4/8/16/28/40 reservoir capacities, prior speculative 8/16/32/48/64 ladder, END-linked prayer charging and 10%-efficient reluctant-prayer rule are superseded.

**Duration-first enchantments approved:** Most installed enchantments should operate **for a defined duration after activation**, rather than requiring a new mana payment per hit or individual contact. Their magical power still comes from their appropriate Leywell and their outward effects still require real physical media where applicable. Exact duration, upkeep, per-effect MP cost, simultaneous effect handling, and activation action rules remain to be approved.

**Mundane media maintenance direction:** Elemental enchantments using ordinary fuel/water should consume **extremely small** physical quantities, with practical refueling after roughly **one day of heavy combat or a week of lighter skirmishes**. Fuel is common and easy to replenish (e.g., lantern oil or water). Its physical presence and sabotage vulnerability remain meaningful, but it is the mana supply that does most of the work. The cadence is the author's approved design direction, not finalized per-activation volume, a universal clock or new purchased supply tariff.

**Not yet approved:** Assistant-proposed minor/standard/powerful Tier I–V activation-cost ladder **1/1/2/2/3**, **2/3/4/5/6**, **4/5/7/10/12** MP respectively; tentative sustained-combat duration of up to ten turns; exact transfer-rounding rules; charging time and any charging action constraints. Do not treat these numbers as canon.

## November 18, 2026 — Enchantment philosophy, real media, and Glassweave Leywells (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Author-approved foundation:** Ordinary enchantments are restrained, purpose-built magical engineering, **not** copies of sophisticated racial Craft spells. Enchanted equipment uses existing magical principles and physical media to execute limited, repeatable functions; it does not grant a wearer mastery of Pyromancy, Wakecraft, or another racial Craft, nor reproduce an elaborate craft technique (for example, a Wakecrafter's independently controlled water-dragon attack). Ordinary enchantments may inject mana into real eligible media to cause basic effects, in a manner broadly comparable to the limited medium-directed application of mana by Warlocks; this comparison does **not** grant Warlock powers or bypass other Crafts' prerequisites.

**Intrinsic enchantments and Anvilcraft:** Anvilcraft's established magical material-working and alteration furnish enchantment's distinctive intrinsic expression. A smith may further alter an item's own properties by enchantment along lines consistent with what Anvilcraft already accomplishes, such as additional magical lightening of a Trufula weapon, without gaining unrelated racial Craft abilities. The scope, magnitude, costs, and precise eligibility of individual intrinsic enchantments remain to be designed; do not infer unrestricted matter transformation.

**Real-medium rule:** Spectacular outward effects must honor genuine medium requirements. An enchanted flaming weapon is not an eternally burning immaterial spell-flame. The author's approved **flaming sword engineering concept** uses (1) a very thin, minimal coating of stored flammable oil dispensed by the weapon, (2) ignition through a tiny **Hearthiron wick**, and (3) mana supplied by an activated **Leywell** to make the established flame burn substantially hotter. The weapon needs **both physical fuel/medium and mana** for its enhanced flaming effect. Its fuel reservoir is a legitimate sabotage or depletion vulnerability; the Leywell is considerably more resilient. This is an approved qualitative construction concept, **not** finalized oil consumption, reservoir capacity, ignition frequency, elemental magnitude, MP draw, or wound mechanics. Other outward enchantments likewise cannot invent required media by merely relabeling their effect as intrinsic.

**Leywell physical form (approved clarification):** Leywells are **not little vials of liquid mana**. They are usually polished **Glassweave orbs or Glassweave cut gemstones**, whose glow visibly communicates the power held within. Their stored magical energy is not a liquid. Preserve existing alternate names *well* and *faithwell*, and already approved capacity/activation rules where separately defined. 'Usually' does not forbid rare exceptional designs; no new universal toughness, price, charging rate, or storage capacity is established here.

**Power source and implements:** Ordinary enchantments **always draw their operating magical power from Leywells**; do not assume they can directly burn the wearer's natural mana reserves or work solely because the wearer has the matching mana color. Future **casting implements** may have narrowly specified exceptions after spell design is settled, but they are specialized **Craft-specific** tools, not universal enchantments usable by anyone with the correct mana color. Do not finalize such exceptions now.

**Preservation and scope:** Established magical colors, medium dependencies, Anvilcraft/Smithcraft prerequisites, physical material effects, Soul Strain rules, and prior approved Leywell-powered mechanisms remain in force. Prior speculative rune/gem-slot counts, active enchantment MP costs, named enchantment families, and catalogue quotas remain **unapproved** until separately authorized. This entry records philosophy and the flaming-weapon example, not a fully calibrated enchantment catalogue.

## November 16, 2026 — Reach distances and Technique-based movement control APPROVED (LATEST)

**Explicit author approval:** Short/Medium melee weapons control only the **1-tile (5-ft)** zone, while Long weapons control only the **2-tile (10-ft)** zone, **not adjacent squares**. Distinguish **attack reach** from **movement control**: Short attacks at 1 tile; Medium attacks at 1 normally or 2 with **one harder hit category** from lunging; Long attacks at 1 or 2 with no innate range-category penalty. A ready, credible defender can require a **Technique movement contest** when an opponent crosses out of its controlled zone, and a Long wielder can contest **entry through its controlled 2-tile barrier into adjacency** by Technique rather than the former SPD thresholds. Success permits lawful continued movement; failure stops at the boundary with no free damage or grapple. Apply **one meaningful strongest-controller contest**, accounting for actual additional threats via existing Pressure principles, without gratuitous multiple checks or unapproved numeric bonuses.

**Explicitly rejected for this change:** automatic grapple on entry, a free/no-action grapple initiation roll, and default attacks of opportunity. Normal grapple initiation remains a committed offensive Technique check under existing action limits. **Superseded:** prior Long-versus-Short 1.25/1.50 effective-SPD reach-control entry gates and old non-distance-defined reach bands. **Unresolved:** exact Technique-ratio d20 bands, Pressure modification to the contest, expenditure/timing and repeated crossing, forced movement and unusual geometry. Do not invent those values. Updated `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`; no WR, attack Power, or armor changes.

## November 16, 2026 — Dash movement-tile multipliers and upward rounding APPROVED (LATEST)

**Explicit author approval:** In the existing two-action physical combat pilot, **Dash (Half Dash, 1 action) increases the character's already determined movement-tile allowance by 50%**, ceil(M × 1.5); **Double Dash (Full Dash, 2 actions) increases it by 100%**, ceil(M × 2), where **M** is the ordinary movement-tile allowance. **Round UP to a whole tile** after the multiplication (for instance, M=5 gives 8 or 10 tiles, respectively). Do **not** re-evaluate SPD-to-tile thresholds or compute extra tiles based on Dash's temporary Combat Speed bonus. Existing **+25%/+50% Combat Speed until the start of the next turn** remains separately operative; initiative, action costs, one-offensive-action limit, reach/terrain restrictions, and other combat mechanics are unchanged.

**Supersession and scope:** Replaces the November 15 pilot's "no movement distance multiplier" language for Dash, without setting a numerical starting tile allowance or a SPD-to-tiles progression. Those base-movement questions remain **UNRESOLVED**; the assistant's previously illustrated 5-tile starting value and any SPD brackets are not newly canonized. Updated 22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md, this changelog and 00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md. No weapon, armor, Technique, or other movement rules changed.

## November 16, 2026 — One Technique check replaces grapple attack initiation (APPROVED; LATEST)

**Explicit author approval:** Grapple initiation is resolved through **a single Technique d20 check**, based on approved **Technique = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX**, replacing the prior ordinary attack-check requirement. A valid reach-entry/physical opportunity is required *before* checking for a hold; no two-stage attack-plus-Technique roll, free attack, injury or automatic adjacency grapple is granted. Initiation is an offensive maneuver under the existing **one offensive attack action per turn** constraint. Prior November 16 notes describing Technique initiation as a proposal are **superseded** on this point only.

**Still open:** exact Technique score-to-d20 difficulty mapping, action points required for initiation or escape, permitted repeat attempts, and precise Pressure shifts/timing. Approved escape failure consequences and Pressure-sensitive escape direction remain. No change to damage, Weapon Rating, Dash or tile movement.

**Changed:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` on `main`.

## November 16, 2026 — Technique unified control statistic; Pressure worsens grapple escape (LATEST)

**Explicit author direction:** Rename/generalize prior approved **Grapple Control `2STR+DEX`** into the broader **Technique statistic `2 × effective STR + effective DEX`**. Grappling, trips, disarms, shoves and throws are non-damaging combat-control Technique concepts; only existing grapple-specific effects are currently specified, and the author deferred all other procedures. **Pressure on the escaper makes grapple escape harder.** Proposed consistent tier mapping: 0 net PP unchanged, 1–2 PP one difficulty category harder, 3+ PP two categories harder; the author approved the direction but **has not expressly selected the exact shift values**. Existing failed-escape immobility and +1 incoming PP continue.

**Awaiting decision:** User asked whether initiation should use attack accuracy or Technique. Assistant recommended one **Technique check**, with existing reach-entry eligibility and no double roll, but the proposal **does not yet supersede the approved attack-check initiation rule**. Escape/initiation action costs, category conversion, PP expiry and stacking remain unresolved. No changes to weapon/armor Power, material, injury, or experimental additive WR.

**Files:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`.

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple Control formula `2STR + DEX` APPROVED (LATEST)

**Author's explicit approval:** Use **`G = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX`** as the controlling combatant's and escaping combatant's grapple-control attribute score. STR has double DEX's influence; the older `STR + DEX` equal-weight proposal is superseded. This is an approved grapple-specific score, not a replacement for ordinary hit/evasion Combat Speed (`SPD + DEX/4`). The exact proposed conversion of control ratios to six Naked-d20 escape categories, initiation/escape action costs, expiration/stacking of the failed-escape +1 incoming PP and other grappling details remain **unapproved experimental matters**. Keep approved grapple-range restrictions, shield bypass and failed-escape consequence intact.

**Files changed:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`, direct to `main`.

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple-range combat constraints and escape direction APPROVED IN PRINCIPLE

**Author's explicit direction:** A true grapple denies ordinary Long-reach melee attacks by the opponent; penalizes Medium-reach melee attacks by **one existing hit-difficulty category**; leaves eligible Short melee and unarmed attacks functional; prevents two-handed ranged weapon attacks in the grapple while permitting physically eligible one-handed ranged weapon use; and permits attacks by the grappler to bypass their held opponent's normal shield interception/block (not END or worn armor). **Grapple initiation requires an actual attack check**, not just adjacency. **Grapple escape must be a d20 check favoring combined STR and DEX** and should use existing accuracy categories where feasible. **Failed escape** leaves the character unable to leave the current square by ordinary movement and incurs **one incoming PP**.

**Still pending the author's numerical approval:** Escape ratio/attribute weighting, action costs and legal timing of initiation/escape, failure-PP duration and repeat stacking, and edge cases such as third-party intervention, forced repositioning or special abilities. `STR+DEX` control and `(holder STR+DEX)/(escaper STR+DEX)` mapped to six-category thresholds is an explicitly labeled **test proposal only**. These changes do not approve experimental additive WR or alter approved shield durability/Power.

**Files changed:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Extreme nonviable END-specialists EXCLUDED from headline balance calibration (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Author's explicit direction:** Stop treating extreme END-focused characters who cannot themselves meaningfully wound more-generalist opponents as mandatory balance targets. Future weapon, armor, Quality and combat calibrations must **exclude those nonviable offense END-maximizers from headline result aggregates, optimizer objectives, and mandatory woundability constraints**. They may intentionally be very durable while being weak or useless at offense; such comedic or poor builds are acceptable in Mystaria. This explicitly supersedes the prior study-level emphasis on ensuring ordinary generalists could wound every dedicated END specialist. Do not omit viable defensive builds or the normal endurance investment of generalists. Optional extreme stress tests belong in separately labeled, non-binding appendices, not main optimization results. Determine exclusion by actual offensive viability against plausible generalists, not merely by the END attribute label. Historical simulations remain preserved as historical results.

**Authority boundary:** Study-scope guidance only. Does not approve the proposed `A² + WR²` formula, 31 numerical WR ladders, alternate wound thresholds, secondary-mode multipliers, material scaling, or any modification of approved live combat canon. Updated `26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` to prevent reuse of superseded optimization objectives.

## November 16, 2026 — Power Attack self-Pressure and single-target identity APPROVED (LATEST)

**Explicit author approval:** Replace the previously approved **+1 harder attack difficulty on Power Attack** with **one self-applied meaningful Pressure Point** lasting **from maneuver initiation until the start of the attacker's next turn**; no inherent Power Attack accuracy penalty remains. Retain **2 actions**, **one offensive attack action**, **×1.25 of otherwise final physical Attack Power**, legal reach/penetration/END/AR/injury calculations and all existing defensive mechanics. **Power Attack is explicitly single-target**, never a free Sweep/Wide Sweep/Line/group attack; it cannot be Quick Attack, Double Strike, or stacked with another offensive attack action. The one self-PP can matter to credible incoming threats according to existing net Pressure tiers; it does not add to the user's offensive Pressure or grant a free counterattack.

**Headshot combination unchanged in Power, updated in difficulty:** A real vulnerable helmet/head gap remains **+2 harder hit difficulty, actual AR25, ×1.25 final Power**. Combining it with approved Power Attack yields **×1.5625 final Power** and the attacker's one self-PP, but **only the head-gap's +2 accuracy difficulty** is imposed, not the formerly additive +3 categories. An actual exposed head uses the existing +1 difficulty/AR0 and no extra headshot multiplier. Fully covered helmet regions without accessible gaps yield no bonus. The earlier November 16 self-Pressure proposal and accuracy-based Power Attack entries below are **superseded**; retained for historical continuity.

**Changed files:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`, committed directly to `main`. **Remaining balance caveat:** Against a target unable to retaliate, self-PP can be less costly than the former hit penalty; ordinary Full Attack retains an advantage with multiple targets and in fight geometries where that matters. Observe in organic tabletop play, not an imposed simulation schedule.

## November 16, 2026 — Headshot precision damage APPROVED; Power Attack self-Pressure alternative UNDER REVIEW

**Latest express approval:** The author approved the headshot-study direction. The only new **headshot-specific physical Power bonus** is **×1.25 of otherwise final physical Power** when a legally declared, genuinely reachable **vulnerable protected helmet/head structural gap** is successfully hit. Existing precision target rules apply: **protected gap +2 harder categories, actual AR25**; **exposed head +1 harder where precision is necessary, actual AR0 and NO headshot bonus**. Fully protected surfaces without actual gaps cannot borrow AR25 or receive a headshot bonus. Tiny exposed openings keep the existing +3 difficulty / AR0 without automatic additional Power. Treat headshots as **single-target** despite a weapon's ordinary group coverage. Genuine anatomy, equipment, line of attack, Shield Guard and all previously approved hit and injury rules remain authoritative.

**Combined approved option:** One eligible 2-action Power Attack can target a legitimate head gap: **×1.25 Power Attack ×1.25 headshot = ×1.5625 final physical Power**, with the **current approved +1 Power Attack accuracy difficulty and +2 gap difficulty** applied separately (total +3). No extra attack, action bypass or unconditional access to helmet gaps results.

**New proposal UNDER REVIEW, not an approved revision:** Change the Power Attack cost from +1 harder accuracy to **1 self-applied incoming Pressure Point until the user's next turn**, analogous to Double Strike. User requests design feedback, not yet a settled numerical replacement. Preserve the existing **+25% Power / +1 harder category / 2 actions** Power Attack until specifically changed. Concern: with no incoming enemy, self-PP costs nothing, and Power Attack risks eclipsing ordinary Full Attack; review together with headshot precision and group-coverage rules.

**Files changed:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` on `main`. Remaining user decision: retain accuracy penalty or approve an explicitly calibrated self-Pressure variant of Power Attack.

**Author approval:** Adopt **Power Attack (+25%)** as a 2-action offensive Full Attack with **one harder hit-difficulty category** and **×1.25 otherwise final physical Attack Power** (after established weapon computation; before Impact). It is one attack action under the existing per-turn limit, and it cannot be combined with Quick Attack or Double Strike that turn. Existing hit categories, legal shield/contact routing, material, armor bypass, END, wound thresholds, injury stages and conditions remain unchanged. The +50% alternative was not approved.

**Headshots remain research:** No +15%, +25%, +35%, or +50% vital-location bonus is canon. Ordinary legally exposed-body (actual AR0, +1 category) and protected-gap (actual AR25, +2 categories) targeting remain. ## November 16, 2026 — Independent Weapon Rating design principles AUTHOR APPROVED; formulas remain proposals

**Latest explicit author approval:** Approve three qualitative principles for possible weapon scaling: **(1)** individually assigned independent Weapon Ratings are worth pursuing; **(2)** weapon design and tactical identity must retain a meaningful Power contribution as attributes increase, without flattening weapons by handling class; **(3)** higher Quality must matter while avoiding excessive wound-severity jumps. The author's instruction is **not** to focus on indefinite scaling now. This approval is not a numerical replacement of existing `Power=(A × F)²`, and does **not** enact the November 16 tested `A² + WR²`, `Fₚ × A² + WR²`, 31 hypothetical WR tables, alternate-mode adjustments, unarmed mechanics, material coefficients, or Quality progression.

**Reasoning boundary:** The November 16 experimental report found **215/3270** unwoundable-even-at-AR-0 fixtures under current Power, **514/3270** under pure additive (regression) and **82/3270** under its shape-coefficient candidate (improvement but not full solution). Current strict level-100 Slashing **STR > hybrid > DEX** occurred **4/13** race profiles, pure additive **0/13**, shape coefficient **4/13**. Those are scoped diagnostic comparisons, not author-approved balance standards or proof that the prior **armor/survivability tradeoff for hybrid versus DEX** failed. The author's previous approval to retain existing physical damage rules pending holistic evidence remains operative. Continue the friends-led tabletop pilot without an AI testing schedule.

**Files updated:** `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md` for weapon-design principles, `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` for Quality and armor-tradeoff scope, `10_Canon_Changelog.md` for this decision, and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` for current status/navigation. **No numerical combat canon changed.** Remaining decisions (only when the author elects): final attack formula, rating ladder, material treatment, special-mode interaction, Quality change, unarmed effect and overall balance implications.

The author approved recording this future design possibility in [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md).

Mystaria’s physical Armor Quality may eventually extend indefinitely beyond Quality V so equipment can continue progressing alongside Heroes above level 100. The existing squared-additive Defense formula (`Defense = END² + effective covered AR²`) imposes no inherent mathematical Quality ceiling. ## November 15, 2026 — Shield Deflect stance RETIRED (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Latest explicit author correction:** **Deflect is removed from the playable Mystaria combat rules, including the current physical tabletop pilot.** It is vestigial and is **not** an available shield stance, action, reaction, alternative Guard mode, or shield-specific one-Pressure-Point counteraction. A shieldbearer may use legal **Guard**, **ordinary shield bash**, or **Light/Medium shield-and-weapon Double Strike** (Heavy shield Double Strike remains prohibited), plus other independently approved actions and prepared **Parry** under their actual rules. **Ordinary offhand Defense with a genuinely usable offhand weapon remains intact** and is *not* renamed, deleted, or granted for free merely by holding a shield. All older passages offering or approving shield **Deflect** in this document or other canon files are **historical and superseded**; historical descriptions are preserved solely to show design evolution. Nothing else about Shield Rating, physical contact routing, durability, magical Guard, Pressure, Double Strike, Parry, Dash or movement is changed. Future balancing questions no longer include whether to keep Deflect.

## November 15, 2026 — Two-action combat, Naked d20 and Candidate A APPROVED for friends-led physical PILOT

**Author approval:** Adopt the assistant's recommended physical combat package for the author's own unhurried play with friends. **No AI-driven playtests, scheduled follow-up, or obligatory logging.** This is **approved for pilot play**, not proof of permanently completed balancing. The author requested future balancing QUESTIONS only.

**Pilot rules:** **Two actions, maximum one offensive attack action per turn**; Full Attack and approved **60%-per-strike Double Strike** each cost **2**, Quick Attack **1** with **full Power but one harder hit category**, no 80%-Power Quick. Naked d20: **Certain automatic, Easy 4+, Average 8+, Hard 12+, Very Hard 17+, Impossible no ordinary roll**. Keep approved Combat Speed calculation but use **wider pilot cutoffs 0.20/0.80/1.25/2.50/5.00** rather than original **0.50/0.80/1.25/1.50/3.00**. Half/Full Parry **1/2 actions, +1/+2 harder hit categories, 1/2 earned PP for an actually prevented would-be body hit**, with specific prepared triggers, shield-first same-die procedure and expenditure cooldown. Half/Full Dash **1/2 actions, +25%/+50% Combat Speed** through start of next turn, without multiplying movement distance or initiative.

**Candidate A physical shields:** Light/Medium **+1**, Heavy **+2** on legitimately defendable Guard lines. One attack die first tests ordinary baseline, then shielded threshold; **natural miss / shield contact / body hit** are distinct. Supersedes older **automatic physical Guard** and conflicting same-line **+0/+1/+1 obstruction** during the pilot, not established shield durability/overflow, magical interception or material properties. Actual geometry, separate simultaneous attacks and Extreme Pressure still apply. **Historical note superseded by latest author decision:** Shield Deflect was retained at this point in the pilot, but the author subsequently removed it entirely; see the latest retirement entry above.

**Deferred questions, not tasks:** low-level wound routes, high-END non-woundability, Heavy shield mirror pacing and wear, material contact-trigger frequency, relative Quick/Full/Double Strike and Parry choices, broader vs original Speed cutoffs, movement/escape/pursuit geometry, shield flanks, magic interface and one-die tabletop usability. The author can revisit these at any time based on actual friend-group play; there is no deadline and no automated combat monitoring.

**Supersession/owning sources:** [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) contains the full approved pilot and deferred questions; [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) owns new pilot shield routing. Older details not specifically changed continue; the simulation ZIP is evidence, not independently imported into GitHub. Changed files: `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`.

## November 15, 2026 — Vesperyn race name and personal naming convention APPROVED

**Latest author approval:** The Scarlands sheepfolk previously called **Lambfolk** during concept development are officially **Vesperyn** (both singular and plural). *Lambfolk* is a superseded development label, not the proper racial name.

**Previously approved naming convention, now filed under Vesperyn:** gentle/pastoral personal birth names; inherited family or ancestral-hearth surnames that may sound gothic, infernal or devotional; and optional earned religious/inquisitorial titles, offices or epithets. These ceremonial forms do not replace the birth/family name and are not compulsory for ordinary Vesperyn. Examples such as *Elspeth Blackwick*, *Mabel Tallowmere* and *Sister Elspeth Blackwick* illustrate the pattern; they do not establish named NPCs, noble lines, church rank rights or a complete additional approved 30-name list.

**Scope and outstanding work:** The Vesperyn concept centers on Scarlands demon hunting, religious inquisitors, sacred flame and Pyromancy. The existing-flame prerequisite of Pyromancy is already independently established in `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`. The user's wider ideas (Hearthkeeper and Ashen Legion institutions, First Flame phenomena, demon classification and possession, relations with Vren Blood Courts, Centaur-city community, proposed wool fire resistance and smoke breathing) await their own detailed lore/mechanics references; the present approval does not assign new SP/MP costs, finalized numerical racial traits, legal powers or playable-race benchmark statistics.

**Updated files:** `24_Racial_Naming_Conventions_and_Namebook.md`, `02_Races.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. Earlier approved 15-group/450-example namebook remains preserved.

## November 15, 2026 — Centaur full equipment attribute bonuses APPROVED

**Latest author decision:** Remove the Centaur racial penalty that halved equipment-derived numerical attribute bonuses. Centaurs now receive **100% of each otherwise legal item's normal stat bonuses**, using the ordinary stacking and rounding rules, including normal Junk accessory fractional pooling. Keep the **10% reduction to aggregate equipment Soul Strain**, rounded up once after totaling equipped sources, and all other Centaur racial properties. This explicitly supersedes the earlier 50%-bonuses rule, including the prior preserved-halving language elsewhere in this historical document. No other race, enchantment coefficient, crafted item or equipped-item bonus is changed. Comprehensive race-comparative balance testing must still include all **13 playable races**, including Centaurs.

## November 15, 2026 — Centaur racial Soul Strain revision and playable test roster APPROVED

**Author's latest explicit decision:** The Centaur racial affinity now grants **only a 10% discount on equipment-derived Soul Strain**, replacing the former 50% discount. Count the normal Soul Strain of all actually equipped items, apply **×0.90 to that aggregate**, and round **UP once** to whole strain points (ceil(0.90 × equipment subtotal)); calculate unrelated sources separately without this discount. Examples: 4 equipment strain ⇒ 4, 12 ⇒ 11, 20 ⇒ 18. This preserves modest meaningful savings across an equipped loadout without a per-item rounding trap. **SUPERSEDED by the author's subsequent November 15 approval:** Centaurs no longer halve numerical attribute bonuses provided by equipment. They receive **100% of the ordinary bonuses** under all normal item stacking, fractional bonus and rounding rules. Preserve all other Centaur traits and mechanics, including natural green MP and the distinct Celestite direct soul-damage exclusion.

**Playtest instruction approved:** Centaurs are established playable Heroes. Include them alongside the previously tested twelve playable races (including Kobold) in **all future comprehensive race-comparative balance tests**, **13 total**. The historical twelve-race results remain historical data, not invalidated on their original assumptions; do not present those studies as already including Centaurs. No wider racial statistics, crafting rank system, or combat rules change.

**Updated sources:** 07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md, 09_Equipment_and_Economy.md, 02_Races.md, 00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md, this changelog and AGENTS.md for future ChatGPT/GitHub workflows.

## November 15, 2026 — Drider starting-stat balance revision APPROVED

**Latest explicit author approval:** Replace level-1 Drider base **19/19/19/19/19** (STR/DEX/END/INT/SPD; base total 95) with **18/18/16/15/15** (base total **82**). Replace the previous **END +25%** species modifier with **+10% to EACH of STR, DEX, END, INT and SPD**, an **express Drider-only exception** to the November 6 general 25%-increment racial modifier standard. New effective level-1 Drider scores are **STR 19.8, DEX 19.8, END 17.6, INT 16.5, SPD 16.5** (effective total **90.2**), replacing the historical **19/19/23.75/19/19** (total **99.75**). This intentionally preserves moderately high STR and DEX and reduces early END-driven durability. Driders are no longer highest-total by default; their extreme rarity does not require the strongest statistical start. Preserve Drider climbing, vibration detection, silk, six legs/two arms, lore, abilities, starting item rules, and +2 base-level advancement/+1 five-level milestones. **All earlier Drider-specific test results using former stats must be marked historical and recalculated before being used as current balance evidence.** No other race is changed; the 25%-increment rule remains in force for other races.

**Updated canon sources:** `01_World_and_Shimmer.md` (owning racial stat table and design rationale), `02_Races.md` (Drider species entry and explicit exception), this changelog, and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. ## November 15, 2026 — DM encounter wound-route check APPROVED; ChatGPT-play priority

**Explicit author approval:** Adopt the early equipment audit's **starter encounter viability check**, and make the requirement **especially explicit for Mystaria played through ChatGPT**. When an intended encounter victory requires the Hero to wound an enemy, the DM or AI DM **must verify in advance at least one credible, accessible wound route** using the Hero's **real legal weapon/abilities, Power, reach, space, enemy END/protection, actual armor coverage and shield rules**. A theoretically exposed AR 0 hit that still cannot cross the strict **Impact >0.75** threshold is not a solution, nor is an unavailable weapon, unattainable shot, invented weak spot or impossible tactical maneuver.

**If none exists**, adjust the opponent or its defenses consistently with canon, provide a legitimate alternative action/resources, or ensure the encounter has an achievable goal not dependent on wounding (escape, negotiation, environmental victory, withdrawal, etc.). **Deliberately invulnerable threats are acceptable when winning does not require injuring them.** This is DM/ChatGPT preparation guidance, not a universal rule that every enemy must be woundable, not a guarantee of success, and not authorization to alter weapon F, armor AR, hit probabilities, innate END or combat thresholds. The November 15 early equipment audit's Drider, Naga and other high-END cases motivate the check, but its test numbers remain report evidence rather than canon mechanics.

**Owning rules:** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, with cross-reference in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` and AI DM reminder in `AGENTS.md`. Ranged starter hand-crossbow and sling Power benchmarks remain an independent open design task.

## November 15, 2026 — Starter armor, Heavy armor availability, and anti-bypass crafting warning APPROVED

**Latest explicit author approval:** The previously proposed early equipment plan is adopted as **normal acquisition expectations**. New Heroes retain their existing granted Junk Quality I Boughsteel weapon and armor ensemble. **Junk Light armor is the ordinary starter choice; legally qualified Medium Junk armor may be used at the DM's discretion; Heavy armor (including Partial, Standard, Full) is absent from ordinary starter grants and beginner shops.** Qualifying Heavy **weapons** remain available to level-1 characters subject to existing requirements, rather than being level-locked like Heavy armor stock.

**Approved progression:** Levels **1–4** Light normally, Medium if qualified; levels **5–9** QI Medium readily available and Partial Heavy uncommon later; levels **10–14** QI Partial/Standard Heavy reasonable specialist gear, Full Heavy exceptional for a qualified character; levels **15–20** QI Full Heavy normal high-end armor for qualified users. Special smiths, adventure rewards, and deliberately exceptional early discoveries are DM-controlled. The stages guide **availability**, not new universal class-level use gates or automatic Gem/item payouts. All item stats, existing material/Quality/coverage prerequisites, pricing, and mobility consequences are preserved.

**Explicit author-added DM warning:** **Do not allow self-crafting to bypass appropriate Heavy armor progression or its pre-existing prerequisites.** Heavy armor is not routinely craftable prior to the suitable campaign access band; earlier crafting or unusual acquisition requires a deliberate DM exception **and all genuine smithcraft, forge, materials, Quality and wearer handling requirements must still be satisfied**. A high STR score, money, or normal forge-hours alone cannot evade these restrictions. No new numerical Anvilcant ranks or hard level gates are silently enacted.

**Remaining to test:** Level-1 race-specific Light/Medium Junk starter legality and attack effectiveness; timing of Partial Heavy acquisition, unusual materials and resulting wound vulnerability; possible future adjustments to accessibility after levels 5, 8, 10, and 15. **Owning documents:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` and `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md`; index updated. No physical damage, AR, requirement, crafting-time, or economic formula changes.

## November 14, 2026 — Prepared defense expiry and mixed-action rounding approved

**Author decision:** The prepared defense expires at the **beginning of the caster’s next turn**, whether used or unused. Protection beyond that point requires casting a separate spell with sufficient duration or eligible maintenance. The caster names the spell and a specific trigger on their own turn, before the attack outcome is known. “The next attack targeting my knight” qualifies. “The next attack that hits my knight” depends on the outcome and is invalid. The defense draws from capacity reserved on the preparation turn. Future-turn capacity cannot be borrowed.

**Mixed actions:** When otherwise legal normal physical actions and magic are combined, halve the applicable Channel Limit and round down. The caster may make at most one attack total that turn. A Bloomkin may fire a crossbow as that attack and prepare an eligible magical shield for a nearby Thornguard under the normal casting requirements.

**Unresolved:** Exact response timing and priority, mixed-action commitment timing and maneuver qualification, and interactions with complete spell seizure’s prior preparation or next-action forfeiture and other specialist rules.

Updated [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and the [Canon Index](00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md).

## November 14, 2026 — Prepared reactive defensive casting approved

**Author decision:** On their own turn, casters allocate their existing Channel Limit between any offensive magic and at most **one specific prepared reactive defensive spell**, within the shared budget for all applicable channeling. Name the spell and declare a narrow trigger in advance. The next attack against a specified target or by a specified attacker qualifies. A generic watch for any ally being attacked and any trigger based on an attack's outcome are invalid. When the trigger occurs, activation requires a legitimate response opportunity, the allowance reserved on the caster's own turn, the appropriate physical medium and access, and normal MP payment. This supersedes the November 13 permission to borrow next-turn capacity.

**Combat and race:** The mixed physical/magical half limit and one-attack cap still apply, along with existing formulas, MP/SP costs, prerequisites and specialist rules. A Thornguard's normal sword strike can coexist with eligible physical shield Guard. Thornguard cannot personally shape mana. A magical shield through a separate eligible caster or powered interface remains conditional on its own approved rules.

**Unresolved:** Half-limit rounding was resolved in the entry above. Exact response timing and priority remain open, along with interactions with complete spell seizure's prior preparation or next-action forfeiture and other specialized rules.

Updated [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and the [Canon Index](00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md).

## November 14, 2026 — Soul anatomy, casting, and ordinary death

**Author-approved expansion:** [Magic and the Soul](01_World_and_Shimmer.md#magic-and-the-soul) now covers Cognum and memory dispersal, Anima affinity and bodily spellcasting, Spiritus damage and Soul Shred, and the Corpus's role in ordinary death and possible early revival. This expands the earlier four-part anatomy entry. The stomach makes managmins bioavailable to the Anima, and the nervous system and especially the hands support spell shaping. The Spiritus binds the parts and reserves, and structural damage can reduce attributes through Soul Shred. Existing Celestite weapon and armor XP erosion and triggers still apply.

**Ordinary death:** The heart stops, and the loss of the Corpus's protection lets formless black mana contribute to soul unraveling as decomposition sets in. Cognum decay eventually disperses memories. Exceptional early healing may permit revival. Unusual component independence remains possible, including established Cognum preservation during Celestial Recall. The Warlock, Moontouched, and Celestial Recall rulings continue to apply.

**Unresolved:** Revival timing and requirements, conditions for independent survival or function of individual parts, how Cognum persists in Soulwork vessels, and any general Soul Shred procedure beyond established sources. Necromancy remains a future concept involving external mana puppeteering a damaged Spiritus, with mechanics undefined.

Updated [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Centaurs and Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md), and the [Canon Index](00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md).

## November 14, 2026 — Warlock and Moontouched Anima and Celestial Recall

**Author decision:** Warlocks have the same four soul parts. Their larger, more powerful **Anima** fills the space in the **Spiritus** where pure mana would ordinarily be stored. They need dietary managmins to survive. Their Anima uses trace amounts of mana to move other mana, always less than 1 MP. Warlocks have **0 natural MP** and draw on external mana to use Manacraft. A Moontouched who is not a Warlock has a shriveled, weak **Anima** that digests only enough dietary managmins to keep them alive.

**Celestial Recall:** Once Celestial Recall begins, the **Astral Heart** reaches across the planes and breaks down what remains of the **Anima**, **Spiritus**, and **Corpus**, including the physical body. The **Cognum** remains intact. The Heart combines the recovered material with formless mana to recreate the other three parts and imbues the recreated soul with the original Cognum. Greater damage, greater power, and greater depletion in the original body each increase the mana required and lengthen Recall. Its exact duration is intentionally unspecified. This supersedes the older days-or-weeks estimate.

Earlier descriptions of Warlocks as empty or entirely without mana now apply only to spendable natural MP. Existing Manacraft costs and handling rules still apply. How Warlock soul vessels preserve disembodied consciousness remains open.

Updated [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Races](02_Races.md), and the [Canon Index](00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md).

---

## November 14, 2026 — Four-part soul anatomy approved

**Author decision:** Anima manipulates and emits magic and converts dietary managmins into pure mana. Cognum is the mind housed in the brain. Spiritus is the stabilized mana framework of the soul and contains pure mana. Corpus is the physical body that anchors Spiritus to this form.

[Magic and the Soul](01_World_and_Shimmer.md#magic-and-the-soul) defines these parts alongside the existing whole-soul description of mana storage and regulation. Existing affinities, racial exceptions, and spell rules continue to apply.

**Unresolved:** How brain-housed Cognum relates to identity and consciousness preserved in a disembodied soul vessel under [Soulwork](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md#rank-v--soulwork), and how the four parts relate to Starspawn Astral Hearts and Celestial Recall. The anatomy of racial exceptions to mana production and shaping is also open.

## November 13, 2026 — Turn-scale Channel Limit and defensive channeling approved

**Historical ruling:** The next-turn borrowing provision below was superseded by the November 14 prepared-defense revision above. The shared throughput and mixed-action rules still apply, with round-down clarified in the November 14 expiry and rounding entry.

**Author decision:** The existing Channel Limit now governs **total channeled mana in a turn**, rather than permitting its full per-effect value repeatedly. A caster can ration offense to retain applicable defensive capacity. After their active turn, permitted defenses may borrow the **next** turn's channeling allowance; mana spent then reduces both their MP reserve and the next turn's remaining channel budget. A caster can be forced onto defense to exhaust next-turn options. These rules do not themselves authorize additional actions, new interrupts, or free repeated reactions.

**Mixed actions:** Characters may combine legal physical actions and spellcasting, but their Channel Limit is **halved** and they can make **at most one attack total in that turn**. Do not stack physical Double Strike or a weapon attack plus a separate offensive spell into two attacks. Existing casting/physical action legality still applies.

**Preservation and open implementation:** Existing attribute-derived Channel Limit formulas, MP/SP costs, class/racial skill ledgers, Wyrmcraft Overchannel/Serpentbreaker and other specialized exceptions, Warlock external-mana/Handling Capacity, physical Guard and attack rules remain unchanged. Still open: exactly when reactive interventions are legal and how they interact with complete spell seizure's action debt, as well as other conditional exceptions. See [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md).

---

## November 13, 2026 — Magical media and spell-seizure rules approved

The author rejects treating “mastery expands access” as a universal Craft progression, replacing it with **Craft-specific medium dependencies**. Wakecraft requires controlled liquid water; ice/vapor require trained recovery before control and may require a recovery action and Channel Limit. Pyromancy can amplify a match, spend flames as ammunition and seize fire into smoke for specialized techniques. Psycraft uses gem-focused Cipherlight, not abundant-light scaling; closed eyes resist but do not immune, and an established psychic effect can persist across interrupted beam contact while the focus survives.

**Contest thresholds:** one existing spell Power statistic, not separate control: equal Power seizes medium (no additional spell on that turn), double Power seizes spell (prepare by sacrificing prior-turn action or surrender next action). Extra MP committed to challenge is spent, even on failure. Medium seizure uses only one's own compatible Craft, not free enemy abilities. Wakecraft may displace/freeze/vaporize seized water; pyromancers inhale fire to produce specialized smoke; psychics dispel an opposing effect and can bolster a third party's will against enemy mind control. Third-party target resistance persists.

**Supersession with preservation:** older direct ice/vapor Wakecraft control, notably literal Threefold Weave, conflicts with newer liquid-only rule and must be redesigned without silently deleting named ranks or SP costs. Existing Naga Psychokinesis and other approved ranks remain historically preserved pending separate remake. Do not invent detailed timing, MP or Power formula, volumes, or completed pyromancy skills. See [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md).
