## November 13, 2026 — Physical ranged benchmarks and shield constructions approved

Use Bludgeoning for Skyvein throws with the ordinary 25% covered-AR bypass on body hits. At the same legal attributes, Light single-target throw Power is **120% of comparable bow Piercing Power** and its range boundaries are **75% of that bow's**. Medium and Heavy use **100% of comparable bow Piercing Power** for one target and **50% of that bow's range boundaries**. Use Shortbow, Recurve, and Longbow as the respective ordinary bow benchmarks for the three classes. The existing 75% per target for two and 55% per target for three apply to the appropriate single-target throw Power. Existing 1/2/3 target caps, independent hit resolution, return, and full-turn loss of shield benefits apply. Exact ordinary bow range distances and detailed return-path rules remain unresolved.

The approved starting full-draw Power benchmarks for **Shortbow / Recurve / Longbow** are **702 / 1,485 / 2,681 Piercing** and **1,048 / 2,218 / 4,005 Slashing** (nearest whole values from the calibration). The corresponding draw caps used are **22 / 32 / 43**. Use the existing quadratic partial-draw rule and ordinary arrowhead bypass. These are working Power benchmarks; ordinary bow range distances remain unresolved.

Approved shield constructions are **Trufula frame with a load-bearing Boughsteel face** (physical Rating ×1.00, support STR ×0.85, with existing applicable Trufula hazards) and **Boughsteel frame with a load-bearing Glassweave face** (Balanced B, including first-physical-wound shatter). A **Nullporcelain inset on a load-bearing Boughsteel face** remains an open design question for magical interception. Boughsteel governs its physical contact. Celestite retains its Quality V triune-forging requirement. Detailed fabrication specifications and hours remain open.

These benchmarks supersede the pending status in earlier Skyvein trials. Files updated are Shield Profiles, Ranged Catalogue, Canon Changelog, and Canon Index.

## November 13, 2026 — Skyvein multi-target Power split approved

The author approved **75% of the applicable single-target throw Power per target for two distinct targets**, and **55% per target for three distinct targets**. Apply the split to final Power before target defenses. The total before defenses is 150% or 165% if all contacts land. Existing Light/Medium/Heavy target caps of 1/2/3 and the full-turn forfeiture of shield benefits apply. Single-target Power and range, especially for Light, remain open. Bludgeoning and half-bow range were test assumptions. See [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md#skyvein-bow-comparison-and-approved-multi-target-split) for the landed-wound test and earlier trial.

## Shield spell interception and Skyvein bow benchmark — November 13, 2026

A usable, qualified Nullporcelain or Glassweave shield in Guard can intercept **any incoming spell effect against its bearer**, including psychic effects and the bearer's exposure to an area spell. Resolve the shield's absorption against that exposure. When the shield absorbs the entire incoming spell Power, the bearer is unaffected. When spell Power exceeds what the shield can absorb, the excess reaches the bearer and the spell resolves against them under its normal rules. An area interception protects only that bearer; the area and its other targets resolve normally. If Guard is unavailable under existing rules, including net Extreme Pressure of at least 3 PP, the spell resolves against the bearer normally. Against two genuine coordinated simultaneous flankers, the existing one-interception limit applies; the unblocked attack resolves normally.

Spell magnitude-to-Power conversion, the exact absorption and overflow calculation, partial-effect adjudication for non-damage spells, and whether magical contact causes ceramic wounds remain unresolved.

**Approved balance target:** Skyvein throws should be comparable in effectiveness to a bow with similar stat requirements, accounting for the ability to hit distinct targets and for losing all shield benefits on the throwing turn. Exact throwing Power, range bands, and return-path details remain proposals until selected. The [shield rules and draft trials](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) own the details.

This supersedes the earlier direct-single-target-only interception rule. Updated Shield Profiles, Combat and Shimmer Guard, Ranged Weapon Catalogue, Canon Changelog, and Canon Index.

## November 13, 2026 — Shield material package and fixed Reprise Bronze Rating approved (LATEST)

**Reprise Bronze:** Calculate one Rating at each Quality using `round_half_up(Boughsteel Medium shield Rating × Reprise Bronze Medium armor AR / Boughsteel Medium armor AR)` with all inputs at that Quality. Quality I–V Ratings are **19/33/53/81/112**, fixed across forms. Select a prepared **Light, Medium, or Heavy** form at the start of the wielder's turn without an action. The form lasts until their next turn and governs handling, Guard obstruction, and class-based attack options, including Heavy's Double Strike restriction. Quality and installed enchantments persist. The shield and worn armor switch independently. Standard Gem cost equals Medium at **30/60/120/240/480 Gems**. All-form forging remains unresolved, and support STR ×1.00 remains a conditional engineering reference.

Balanced B applies to legal load-bearing ceramics. **Glassweave** uses **×1.25 physical Rating, ×0.95 support STR, ×1.75 magical Rating**. **Nullporcelain** uses **×1.15 physical Rating, ×0.75 support STR, ×3 magical Rating**. Apply the magical multiplier to Rating before squaring for eligible Guard contacts. First-physical-wound shatter and breaking-blow overflow still apply.

Ready **Bellcopper** multiplies final physical shield Defense by **×1.50 Medium / ×2 Heavy**. Actual condition loss after enhanced Defense charges the reservoir, disabling the multiplier until **three uninterrupted qualifying quiet turns** pass. Light shield contact is acoustically muffled. **Hearthiron** Light/Medium/Heavy bash Fire-effect budgets are **10%/20%/35%** of ordinary final bash Power. **Thornspring** holds **one Springload**, which can be spent on a melee block for **one opposing PP for one turn**, or on a bash for **+10% otherwise final bash Power**.

Open rules include magical spell eligibility and Power matching, whether magic can wound ceramic shields, Skyvein range and throwing Power, Bellcopper acoustic scope and qualifying quiet-turn criteria, Hearthiron elemental conversion and heat economy, Thornspring winding, recharge and Pressure refresh, Celestite weapon Leydrain radius and cutoff, and special-material construction, sourcing, and shield fabrication hours.

This supersedes the previous per-form Rating table, its open standard Gem price, the earlier conditional ceramic factors, and the previous pending status of the approved numbers. The trial entry below records the prior state.

**Files changed:** `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md`, `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`.

**Author-approved shield adaptations:** Nullporcelain gains exceptionally multiplied magical-energy Shield Rating and eligible single-target spell/psychic interception while legally in Guard; Glassweave gains the smaller version. Both obey the latest automatic-Guard/Extreme-Pressure/synchronized-flank constraints and first-physical-wound ceramic shatter, not blanket spell immunity. Skyvein Light/Medium/Heavy shields may be thrown at **1/2/3 distinct targets and return** at the cost of **ALL shield benefits for that entire turn**. Reprise Bronze shield Rating must match the ratio of Reprise Bronze AR to Boughsteel AR at the *same class and Quality*, not old ×0.90 constant, and gains the same start-of-turn no-action Light/Medium/Heavy prepared-form change as its armor. Bellcopper shield gains the resonant defensive-reservoir material identity. Hearthiron **shield itself** retains **×3 Fire resistance**, with size-scaled fire-enhanced bashes requested for proposal. Thornspring shield stores Springloads for melee-block **Recoil as one-turn opposing Pressure** and **+10% final shield-bash Power per Springload deliberately spent**; it does not inherit the suit's dash boost. Celestite shield receives **weapon-equivalent Leydrain** and is expressly a story-warping **QV-only** triune-forged item; normal play is not intended to grant all three of weapon/shield/armor, or routinely even one. ## November 13, 2026 — Ceramic first-wound shatter; Junk two-state 90% Defense; automatic Guard; qualified half-time smith repair APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest author explicit revisions supersede the earlier Work-shield report where inconsistent.** **Ceramic load-bearing shields** must be highly defensive for their effective STR requirement but **shatter on the FIRST qualifying physical wound**. Operational contact threshold `P≥D`, where `D=Shield Rating²`, now triggers immediate shattering with no Dented/Cracked ceramic stage. P<D doesn't wound; existing `max(0,P−3D)` only-on-breaking shield torso overflow still applies. This **REPLACES** the older 0.75D/1.75D multi-loss shatter bands from the report. The physical ceramic defense-versus-STR target is approved, but **NO new Nullporcelain or Glassweave shield Rating/STR numbers** were provided; the former optional Nullporcelain ×0.35 and Glassweave ×1.00 Rating references are not satisfactory final ceramic calibration and await author-reviewed alternatives. Keep ceramic BODY ARMOR's own approved wound-severity shatter procedure separate.

**Junk QI Boughsteel shields:** only **two usable conditions** `Intact→Dented→Broken`, rather than ordinary `Intact→Dented→Cracked→Broken`. Junk Shield **Defense Power is exactly 90%** of the matching ordinary QI Boughsteel Shield Defense `Rating²`, NOT 90% of Rating. Use normal 0/1/2/3 shield contact loss bands evaluated on that reduced Defense and cap the loss at Junk's two-loss capacity, applying original only-on-break residual `max(0,P−remaining_Junk_losses×D_Junk)`. General approved Junk costs half matching QI: Light shield 7.5 Gems, Medium/Heavy15 Gems; no unwarranted shield-specific Junk weapon/armor hit-category debuff is invented.

**Guard auto-blocks legal physical hits:** While a genuinely usable shield is in Guard and defender is below **Extreme Pressure net3 PP**, otherwise successful **physically interceptable melee/ranged attacks automatically go to the shield** with no separate block roll, awareness/reflex/reactive-timing or ordinary facing bookkeeping. The combat attack itself must still be a valid hit. This overrides prior routine shield angle-selection, manual interception and precise-projectile timing limitations. A pair of actually **flanking attackers with adjacent initiative places may choose simultaneous attacks**; defender automatically guards exactly one selected threat and the other resolves normally against intended body defenses, not as a free hit. Previously approved voluntary delay for synchronized coordinated attacks remains available. Actual Deflect and Light/Medium Double Strike explicitly surrender active blocks; Heavy shields cannot Double Strike. Guard obstruction +0/+1/+1 remains conditionally applicable only without double counting on the very same shield-contact defense. Extreme Pressure retains its existing restriction; actual incidental collisions with shield are still physical.

**Interim all-equipment smith repair:** A smith able to craft the very item may repair a recoverable/damaged physical object for **HALF that item's lawful complete manufacturing time** and restore its usable condition. Known ordinary weapon repair Light/Medium/Heavy4/8/12h, armor16/20/28h. Standard shield manufacturing time was not ratified; its absolute repair hours await that crafting decision. Repair expense, materials, workforce/market price, salvage and related services are explicitly moved to **MUNDANE CRAFTING AND ECONOMY** backlog. **Ammunition cannot be repaired, except actual reusable throwing weapons**. Existing ordinary ammo forging batches and storage remain unchanged.

**Future Proficiency/verification scope:** Called shots aimed specifically at hands, shield straps and equipment weak points are for future **Proficiency-system** design, not a free current targeted attack; existing exposed-body/armor-gap precision canon remains. End-to-end realistic combat probabilities and fully integrated balance audit are **DEFERRED UNTIL AFTER ENCHANTMENT DESIGN**, which may then be used as an explicitly approved balance lever if needed. Conditional equipment/impact simulations remain historical evidence; no random hit odds or new enchantment parameters approved.

**Special material review:** Work's appendix provided mostly defensive Shield Rating/STR multipliers, NOT shield-specific Skyvein wings, Reprise Bronze form shift, Bellcopper resonance, Hearthiron heat, Thornspring Springload, Oathsilver oath attunement, Nullporcelain/Glassweave comprehensive MR, or Celestite Leydrain/Soul Shred. All suit-only material special powers remain bound to their proper construction. Designing appropriate unique shield versions remains a future author decision, not retroactively approved by the test table. The full detailed new rules and status boundaries are in `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md`.

**Files changed:** `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md`, `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md`, `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, `27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. Remain unresolved only: exact high-defense ceramic Rating/STR profiles, uncommon material shield identity/recipe exceptions, Junk targeting/Proficiency details, shield **fabrication** base workload, material/repair costs and unusual nonrepairable structural loss. No extra rules for charm, magic-ammunition or enchantments are granted.

## November 13, 2026 — Work shield core numeric package AUTHOR APPROVED and published (LATEST)

**Source and explicit author decision:** After a comprehensive Work shield decision report (evaluated 11 races, 6 builds, every level1–100 and 5 Qualities against main at `57e1632`), author requested: **“let's follow its suggestions”**. This approves its recommended **core physical shield numbers and qualified material/shatter extension**, not an invented physical shield recipe, magical effect or assumption of full encounter win probabilities. The report and regression suite reside in the author's Library; those external scripts/CSV have not been added to this GitHub repository by this documentation change.

**Approved Boughsteel Quality I–V Shield Ratings:** Light **18/32/53/78/105**, Medium **24/43/71/104/140**, Heavy **31/55/90/135/180**. QI base Boughsteel STR/DEX before Quality and approved synergy: Light **10/12**, Medium **27/14**, Heavy **62/16**. True active Guard body-line obstruction **Light+0, Medium+1, Heavy+1**, one category only where actual face/edge physically interferes with a legal body-target line, no free full-body cover or +2 Heavy. Shield Defense `D=Rating²` independent of END, body AR or body-armor bypass. Standard physically intercepted attack Power `P`: **0 losses below D; 1 at D–<2D; 2 at 2D–<3D; 3 at ≥3D**. Dented/Cracked fully functional and only-on-break overflow **`max(0,P−remaining_losses×D)` against actual torso defense** remain canon. No separate shield HP.

**Approved core defensive materials:** Boughsteel Rating/STR **1.00/1.00**, Trufula **0.90/0.60**, Adamantite **1.15/2.50**; round computed shield Rating nearest whole halves up before squaring, handling STR up once after class × material × Quality × sustained approved armor synergy, DEX up once after Quality (no material STR multiplier to DEX). Existing 75% eligibility, largest-shortfall SPD penalty and original material vulnerabilities remain. Extra special-material/face-and-frame rows in `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md` are **conditional physical references ONLY FOR INDEPENDENTLY LEGAL BUILDS**, not wholesale approval of new composite recipes, suit-only powers or Celestite earlier than QV.

**Approved narrow ceramic shield-only exception:** Legal **load-bearing ceramic face**: 0 losses through `0.75D`; 2 losses when `0.75D<P≤1.75D`; 3 when `P>1.75D`. Keep actual ceramic armor shatter and material logic unchanged; no extra Power created from a ceramic break and no healing restoration. Decorative ceramic insets do not change a nonceramic load-bearing face's damage class.

**Reaffirmed unaffected earlier approvals:** Shield SPD **0%** by class, full approved shield prices **Light 15/30/60/120/240; Medium/Heavy 30/60/120/240/480 Gems**, all legal Guard/Deflect and standard single `STR F1.05` shield bash; Light/Medium 60%-each shield+weapon Double Strike allowed under real action conditions, Heavy cannot Double Strike. Shield bash does **NOT** automatically receive shield material/Quality offensive-F bonuses absent a separately approved striking implement. Worn armor, race stats, initiative, existing Combat Speed, other weapon Fs and special material manufacture limitations unchanged.

**What remains UNRESOLVED after report:** Junk shield details; attacking straps/hand/weak points; shield repair, restoration and smithcraft workloads (8/16/24 candidate NOT adopted); complete projectile reaction sequencing and extreme attack angles; special-material legal shield construction recipes and elemental/Acid interactions; unique material/specific bash augmentation; exact historical ranging-projectile Power where example arrows used illustrative values; dice hit probabilities, complete equipment material matrix and actual randomized encounter outcomes; archive regression scripts not yet in repo. These require later author-approved decisions, not retroactive canonization.

**Changed files:** `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md` (new owning numeric item tables), `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` (approved combat resolution), `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (item table and unchanged Gem economics), `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md` (bash and independent defense cross-reference), `10_Canon_Changelog.md` (this entry), `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` (navigation/status). Older S30/50/75, QV160/225/270, lower STR gates, Heavy +2 obstruction and unapproved durability tiers are superseded **for the specific now approved physical shield numbers**. No new rules for enchantments, full attack hit odds, physical ammo or repair services.

## November 13, 2026 — Double Strike 60%, shield bash F1.05 and Guard/Deflect/Double Strike APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest explicit approval:** User says the entire preceding Double Strike/shield combat recommendation is “perfect. Approved.” This **canonizes** the selected 60% final physical Power for EACH of two independently resolved, actually qualified main/offhand attacks within ONE action; one legal target per hand, same opponent or two separate reachable foes, without multiplicative double Sweep/Wide Sweep/Line. The maneuver forfeits normal offhand **Attack +1 credible PP** and normal **Defense counter-pressure** mode, and causes exactly **one self-imposed Pressure Point** from declaration **until start of the user's next turn**. Apply real existing PP tier effects, not automatic hit odds, bonus actions or twice the same Pressure. Weapon-specific scaling, F, material, Quality, valid enchantment and actual damage form/penetration belong to EACH weapon's own separate hit.

**User's crucial clarification:** The intended previous offhand 'dagger problem' meant **no incentive to invest in expensive offhand quality/material/enchantment under the old abstract Attack/Defense tactical choices**. A cheap QI dagger and premium high-end offhand were almost equivalent when only furnishing the PP Attack/Defense role, aside from being able to use the weapon's distinct attack form. **It does not mean that a dagger was too strong in a normal separate attack or that daggers should be banned or penalized.** Approved Double Strike creates an optional real hit that makes the second weapon's physical and properly installed magical specifications valuable. Normal offhand Attack/Defense is preserved as an alternative.

**Latest Shield Bash/defensive modes approved:** Ordinary bash **STR-led F1.05, Bludgeoning Narrow, normal Bludgeoning 25% AR bypass**, compared against the already approved STR one-handed mace F1.17. A shieldbearer using **Guard** may use ordinary main attack OR **ONE shield bash as their attack action**, and **still legally actively block/obstruct** under existing angle/pressure restrictions. In **Deflect**, sacrifice deliberate active shield interception and shield-body hit-obstruction benefit to take normal offhand **Defense** credible **one incoming PP counteraction** instead. In **shield+weapon Double Strike**, use 60%-final-Power main attack and 60%-final-Power shield bash, but give up deliberate block, shield obstruction/deflection and offhand Defense, and acquire the same one self-PP until next turn. Real incidental contact against positioned shield remains physical, not active interception.

**Supersession/boundaries:** The immediately preceding November13 combat draft's chosen **60%, F1.05 and stance/action limits are now approved**; its other trial factors **50/55/65/70%**, alternate shield bash Fs, conditional wound benchmark results and invented deterministic hit odds are NOT new canon. Older 'no two actual offhand attacks' applies to non-Double-Strike modes only. Shield shape candidates **S30/50/75**, obstruction **+1/+1/+2**, STR/DEX item minima, material Rating scaling and proposed impact-to-durability loss table **remain unapproved tests**. Preserve shield durability **3-loss states** and **already-approved remaining-absorption overflow**, equipment shortfall and extreme-Pressure limitations. **Finished item Gem prices and Light-shield premium approved earlier today are unchanged.**

## November 12, 2026 — Physical forge time, Anvilcant crew assistance, END workday, Shimmer weapon/arrow capacity APPROVED (LATEST)

**Express author approval and new directives:** The author approves the preceding STR/STR+DEX/DEX armor-tradeoff analysis and says not to adjust the currently approved damage formula on that evidence. The author separately **approves a new concrete physical forging and Shimmer storage framework**.

**Inventory:** One personal weapon occupies **one Shimmer storage inventory slot**; approximately **30 ordinary arrows** (30 normal single-arrow attacks) fit in **another**. This respects the prior six free Shimmer slots and their upgrades, and must NOT be confused with the eleven distinct equipped-gear positions. Existing large nonpersonal object limits, and non-arrow ammo stack sizes, are not overwritten.

**Forge:** Crafting requires a **dedicated real forge** or **magical portable forge item**. Base 8-hour workdays: Light/Medium/Heavy weapons **1/2/3** days (**8/16/24** hours), Light/Medium/Heavy armor **4/5/7** days (**32/40/56** hours), ammunition **up to 120 pieces in one 8-hour batch**. Anvilcant-trained assistance reduces required work hours **25% for one assistant** or **50% for three**; these are not additive. For every **complete 25 END**, the worker can extend daily work by one hour beyond 8, capped at **16 hours/day**. Shorter workdays provide less project progress and therefore stretch calendar completion. Actual production must still qualify under existing crafting, material, Quality and rare Celestite provisions.

**Operational calculation:** total project labor = listed base hours × relevant approved assistant factor (.75 for 1, .50 for 3, otherwise full baseline where no assistance applies); workday upper limit = min(16, 8 + floor(END/25)); carry partial work hours across days. No invented bonus for exactly two assistants or additional beyond three, no free magical portable forge, no new rank gates, crafting costs, magical equipment effects, exotic ammunition recipes or temporary-stat exceptions.

Previous attack-F/weapon-attribute gates, armor AR and defense mechanics stay unchanged.

Historical test tables in the 26-file are preserved and labeled historical. This approval does not ratify all previously draft shape/attack-mode assignments, all other Light/Medium individual gates, or missing crafting/availability effects.

**AUTHOR'S NEW DAMAGE DESIGN PHILOSOPHY AND MANDATED TEST:** The author explicitly changed the intended ranking for best legal Slashing weapons from the former *STR+DEX should be best* design ambition to **STR-only (highest by a meaningful margin), STR+DEX (middle), DEX-only (lowest),** with DEX compensated by ranged combat versatility and anticipating enemy maneuvers. They requested comparison at **levels 1, 10, 20, 40, 60, 80 and 100**, with each build taking the highest-Power eligible Slashing weapon for its attributes. This replaces a design **goal**, NOT the author-approved `A_Both=0.65(STR+DEX)` attack formula, individual weapon F table, racial strengths, injury thresholds, SPD evasion, or ability design. **No numerical combat rebalancing changes are approved from testing**; recommendations remain exploratory. The new target is a design intention, with race-specific exceptions still to be discussed.

**Method and primary source:** Complete 11-race × 7 levels × 3 stat builds = **231 stat-and-weapon optimizations** on identical race base/racial multiplier and equal total allocated growth, using approved Heavy Moderated B gates, Light/Medium November6 test gates, ordinary QI Boughsteel total F, and a real best eligible legal Slashing attack (including actual alternate cut only when built). Build investments every level-up: +2 STR, +1 STR/+1 DEX, or +2 DEX; +1 all base every fifth level. All current eleven Hero races tested, including Nov8 Kharak. Count rankings by race, not claimed actual sampled fights. Same-race **level1 all tie** because no distinct allocated level gains yet. Item ownership, purchasing, special armor, skill bonuses, under-qualified swing tactics, special moves, hit frequencies and actual action cycles are NOT inferred.

**Thornguard raw conditional landed single-Slash Power at L1/10/20/40/60/80/100 (STR / hybrid / DEX):** L1 **1,088 / 1,088 / 1,088**; L10 **5,213 / 3,196 / 2,966**; L20 **12,996 / 6,635 / 5,940**; L40 **39,046 / 17,239 / 14,957**; L60 **79,073 / 32,811 / 28,064**; L80 **133,079 / 53,352 / 46,742**; L100 **201,063 / 78,861 / 71,182**. This strong-STR race follows the desired strict ranking at every later checkpoint; the L100 STR lead over hybrid is ~**155%**, hybrid over DEX ~**10.8%**. STR ultimately favors STR-led F1.52 Greataxe, hybrid Both F1.42 Zweihander, DEX F1.15 Sabre. No unusual damage multiplier was added.

**All-race strict-target success at L10,20,40,60,80,100:** **8/11, 8/11, 3/11, 3/11, 3/11, 3/11**. STR leads in **8/11, 8/11, 9/11, 9/11, 9/11, 9/11**; DEX exceeds the hybrid in **2/11, 3/11, 7/11, 8/11, 8/11, 8/11**. **Drider neutral-STR/DEX example at L100:** STR Greataxe **129,773**, hybrid Zweihander **64,897**, DEX Sabre **74,284**, i.e. actual **STR > DEX > hybrid**. Drider L40 hybrid and DEX both have lawful Zweihander A from equal sum132, identical Power **~14,844** despite different STR/DEX balance. Bloomkin (STR−25%, DEX+25%) and Corvyn (STR−25%) have DEX strongest at L100, race-specific exceptions to any claimed universal ordering. At L100 median best legal single-Slash Power among these eleven race references is **STR127,592 > DEX73,035 > hybrid62,567**, reversing the intended *second and third* ranks on this crude summary. Across actual races, the STR/hybrid relative margin depends heavily on starting racial bonuses and gear.

**Sensitivity:** Hypothetical best eligible Boughsteel QI or QV Adamantite with applicable class material F ×1.15/1.20/1.25 and valid equipment STR/DEX gates still has L100 strict target **3/11** and DEX above hybrid **8/11**. This is not a claim all such expensive items can be acquired on schedule. In fully open actual forward-arc **squad aggregate** scenarios Greataxe/Zweihander Wide Sweep (up to 3, ×2 aggregate) versus Sabre Sweep (up to2, ×1.5 aggregate) can favor hybrid over DEX in a case where per-target Power does not; never call aggregate squad Power guaranteed individual damage/extra actions. All these attacks are Slashing, so equal target covered-AR and END scaling do not reverse raw Power rank, but real hit chance is unresolved and formal wounds follow approved Impact thresholds only on actual contact.

**UNAPPROVED design options:** The current Both A formula is **SUM-ONLY**, so simply nerfing the Sabre will not reliably differentiate qualified high-level DEX-only and hybrid users of the same Zweihander. A hypothetical `A_new_Both=0.85×effective STR+0.65×effective DEX` while keeping weapons unchanged yields strict order in **51 of the 66 race×level L10–100 cases**, up from **28/66**, but raises Both damage significantly and **would change the benchmark Greatsword thrust Impact ~1.635 to ~2.27 at L20**, overrunning the author's earlier ~1.6 target and potentially armor/injury calibration. This proposed formula is **NOT CANON OR OPERATIVE**, and must be comprehensively rebalanced with weapon Fs/form goals if ever considered. More modest/form-specific balanced-stat options can be tested instead. Even with this candidate the STR-disadvantaged Bloomkin/Corvyn may remain racial exceptions. DEX's ranged/predictive combat strengths cannot receive fabricated damage-equivalent percentages.

**Canon status and action:** Heavy Moderate B handling stat gates **APPROVED live main**; STR > hybrid > DEX as a **requested design goal**, not a guaranteed mechanical rule; Slashing damage test and any alternative formulas **EXPLORATORY ONLY**. Existing primary Fs, blade-tip×0.80, purpose-built spike/beak×0.90, genuine Piercing50% AR bypass, forms, real geometry, actions, armor, Guard, racial multipliers and original Both `0.65(STR+DEX)` unchanged. Separate author decision required to alter the stat-Power formula or finalized individual weapon Fs. Full tests with complete race-specific L100 table and specific alternatives in `26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md`; owning actual approved Heavy minima and F in `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, equipment overview in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, and current index in `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`.

**Files changed:** `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`; `26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md`; `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`; `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`; `10_Canon_Changelog.md`; `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. Full older evidence retained under newer authoritative entries, with superseded status. **No further user action needed to record approval**, but any new Power/formula change needs explicit acceptance.

**Author's explicit direction:** Author approved Quill's **plan** to lower the most demanding proposed Heavy-weapon STR/DEX prerequisites to a moderate middle-ground, compare the earlier lower and high proposals across race/stat builds/materials/Qualities, and **leave the already approved all-31 weapon primary F and proper secondary F values unchanged** unless independent issues are found. The author did **NOT** approve any specific new prerequisite numbers in this message. The November6 author-accepted lower replacement minima remain **working test baselines**, and ALL newer Moderate A, Moderate B and previous High minimums remain **UNAPPROVED numeric proposals**; no finalized weapon prerequisite levels have been set.

**Proposed preferred Moderate B QI Boughsteel Heavy stat minimums for future author decision (NOT CANON):** Greatsword STR23/DEX15; Zweihander STR25/DEX16; Greataxe STR24; Maul STR24; Great hammer STR25; Poleaxe STR22/DEX15; Halberd STR22/DEX15; Glaive STR21/DEX14; Pike STR20/DEX14; Long spear **STR16 OR DEX16 with Support STR8 in DEX mode**; Lance STR24; Heavy flail STR25. Alternative more accessible Moderate A and the high stress-test table remain side-by-side with these in **`26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md`**.

Level1 ordinary QI Boughsteel Heavy eligible forms under **old lower / A / B / earlier High**: Thornguard **12/6/3/1**; Vren **12/6/5/2**; Kharak **12/12/11/5**; Moontouched **1/1/1/0**; Bloomkin **1/1/1/0**; Drider **9/2/1/0**. All eleven races qualify for **at least one Heavy form (Long spear)** under proposed B; the High candidate shuts out **seven of eleven** at starting stats. Under proposed B, Thornguard QI starts with Glaive/Pike/Long spear, and its balanced STR+DEX investment meets all twelve Heavy thresholds by level3. Moontouched balanced qualifies greatsword at L11, Zweihander L13, Pike L9; Bloomkin balanced reaches them L17/L20/L14. Specialization affects legal progression: a strictly +2 STR or +2 DEX Thornguard first meets proposed B Zweihander at L10 rather than balanced L3. No item availability or level lock inferred.

**Quality/material sensitivity (Moderate B only):** Starting Thornguard qualifies **3 QI Boughsteel / 3 QII Boughsteel / 12 hypothetical QV Boughsteel / 8 QI Trufula / 0 QI Adamantite** Heavy forms. Moontouched **1/1/1/5/0**, Kharak **11/11/12/11/0**, Vren **5/6/12/12/1**, Drider **1/2/6/12/0**. Existing working material STR/support test factors Boughsteel×1/Trufula×0.5/Adamantite×2.5 and separate workmanship effects apply **once**, with the final requirement rounded up and real fractional effective racial STR not floored. Hypothetical QV item ownership at L1 is NOT implied by a stat gate or economic affordability. Starting Junk is only QI Boughsteel and has no QV threshold discounts.

**Judgment:** **Recommend Moderate B for later author approval**, not A nor the earlier High table, because it reduces early unrestricted Heavy access while preserving a viable Long spear for every documented race and keeping Zweihander only mildly harder than Greatsword relative to their approved F1.42 vs F1.40 (~2.88% raw Power difference at same A). No new values approved by test itself. All existing approved primary Fs, legitimate secondary F factors/mode ratings, 50% ordinary Piercing bypass, armor, racial stats, fixed global +10 base, 0.65 Both formula, attack geometry, Shimmer Guard, Junk starter and pricing are unchanged.

**Repo scope:** `26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md` is the full exploratory test owner; `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md` includes the unapproved B candidate pointer; `10_Canon_Changelog.md` and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` record the latest non-approval test status. This does not write unapproved gate values into the catalogue's operative requirement column. **Required user decision before any gate replacement:** explicitly approve Moderate B as a new *working threshold table* (or approve a different variant) after review. Beyond that, final mode assignment, geometry/grips, unusual character anatomy, exact underqualification effects and complete weapon stat-minimum canon are still separate open areas.

## November 12, 2026 — Author-conditioned secondary blade-tip Piercing Power reduction APPROVED; greatsword/maul/pike geometry battery

**Latest explicit author direction:** Carry out varied open combat, corridor, formation, reach, armor and defensive-stat tests on greatsword versus maul versus pike. **Only if** secondary Piercing appeared disproportionately capable, reduce its **Power without changing armor penetration**, targeting greatsword secondary Impact **approximately 1.6** in the existing class/shape comparison and about midway between the broad Slashing strike and dedicated Piercing. Tests found that working L20 greatsword secondary **1.707** Impact was **too close** to the dedicated pike's **1.798** despite the greatsword's additional Wide Sweep option (its Slash **1.408**) in the representative B-attack versus E-defense/QII Full Medium AR52 contest. Not a universal proven greatsword overpowered verdict, but enough *role overlap* to justify the author-conditionally authorized modest correction.

**NEW APPROVED BLADE-TIP RULE:** Replace the November11 specific **secondary blade-tip Piercing ×0.82 of its already-adjusted primary F** with **×0.80** (64% primary raw Power rather than 67.24%; a **~4.82%** reduction from previous secondary raw Power), applied **once**. The tested greatsword thrust yields **Impact1.624** in the same designated reference, very close to target 1.6 and between Slash1.408 and pike1.798. An exact 1.6 would require ×~0.794, rejected as needless precision for this balance purpose. No other specific Power multiplier change is authorized.

**UNCHANGED:** All legitimate ordinary primary/secondary Piercing retain **50% covered Armor Resistance bypass**; earned actual rear-hit improvement **+10 percentage points** remains; purpose-built secondary **spike/beak ×0.90** remains, as does the general applicable other-secondary ×0.90 default, original greatsword shape ×0.875, pike dedicated shape ×0.90, Bludgeoning/Slashing/primary Piercing forms, armor AR, STR/DEX/END/SPD, Shimmer Guard, class/movement/accuracy rules, material factors, hit probabilities not designed, real geometry and item purchase prices. No blade obtains a Point thrust without a mechanically plausible tip. Do not multiply draft full weapon F by already-incorporated class/shape factors.

**Full battle battery:** Screening every level1–20 with STR+DEX B attacker vs S/E/B defenders, Quality I→II Light/Medium/Heavy where stat eligible yields **81 legal reference encounters**, with **all 81 reduced raw conditional Impact**, **4** losing one injury stage (L18 S/B QII Medium from 3→2; L20 S/B QII Heavy from 2→1). **Not an accuracy or victory-rate statistic.** At L20 END43/QII Medium, ratios are **GS Slashing1.408**, **old GS thrust1.707**, **new GS thrust1.624**, **pike Piercing1.798**, **maul Blunt1.639**. Against QII Heavy, **0.706/1.179→1.122/1.242/0.934** respectively; the slash has less armor penetration, while new Point is still useful. Actual earned rear attacks, exposed/joint weakpoints, broader L10/L16/L25/L50 cases and Heavy Adamantite versus Boughsteel sensitivities were tested. Special purpose beaks/spikes in draft Medium weapon shapes land at **~81–82.4% of dedicated Piercing Power** versus comparable purpose-built war pick, so they did **not** require a blanket reduction.

**Battlefield role outcomes:** Open arc of three accessible frontal foes, sufficient swing clearance: greatsword and maul Wide Sweep up to three (squad-only aggregate ×2); pike Line only one unless aligned. Tight corridor with three unobstructed depth-aligned targets: greatsword primary Wide Sweep and maul Wide Sweep are **not legal due clearance**, greatsword Point may threaten one, pike Line can threaten up to three **only when each is physically accessible and unblocked**. Shield or front obstruction limits line access; no penetration or aggregate benefit from a blocked target. A real established Short grapple inside Long reach defeats both greatsword and pike ordinary Long strikes; maul's Medium reach is case-dependent, not automatically effective. Long-vs-Short defender SPD ratios **1.25/1.50** still control harder/unsupported approach opportunities, with pressure exceptions and real armor mobility burdens. No new attack actions, individual-target Power multiplication or hit probabilities.

**CRITICAL individual-F qualifier:** The F1.395625 greatsword Slash and F1.1165 new Point are **working class/form/shape normalization**, NOT acceptance of the old draft **whole-item F1.27**. If that different unapproved single total F1.27 were selected, its new secondary F would be **1.016** and the example Impact **~1.345**, illustrating that the precise 1.624 target is reference-specific, not universal. Full individual Fs, handling requirements and final weapon catalog balancing remain open; the modest ×0.80 blade-tip rule is the approved global exception. Long-term actual win rates cannot be inferred without an approved evasion success system.

**Files updated directly to main:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (comprehensive test and operative rule), `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` (operative combat rule), `25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md` (shared rule and eight rederived **still-unapproved** individual draft secondary F displays), this changelog and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` (latest authority pointer). The preceding November12 three-path results using ×0.82 remain preserved as **historical pre-adjustment reference**, not operative new factor.

## November 12, 2026 — Three-path physical equipment balance tests recorded (EXPLORATORY; no new rule approval)

**Author direction:** The author approved continuing with all three proposed balancing paths: (1) physical weapon forms and secondary Piercing, (2) armor Quality versus protective class, and (3) STR/SPD, STR/END and STR/DEX character investment. This is **approval to test and document findings**, not approval of any new F, AR, accuracy distribution, equipment price or progression requirement.

**Governing supersession respected:** Already-approved genuine primary AND secondary Piercing uses **50% covered-AR bypass**. Blade-tip secondary **×0.82 of adjusted primary F**, purpose-built spike/beak **×0.90**. Greatsword shape adjustment **×0.875** and pike primary shape factor **×0.90** apply once. Older greatsword thrust ×0.90/25%-AR-bypass and specialized 65% Piercing were NOT reinstated. All absolute item F values remain calibrated working references pending individual author approval.

**Test findings (detailed reproducible setup in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`):**
- **Attack forms:** At common Medium-reference F, a generic blade-tip thrust derived from Slashing generates **~0.44% more conditional landed raw Power** than a same-class shape-neutral dedicated Piercing reference, and shares its 50% AR bypass. This is a **WATCH** for universal near-equivalence, not a new license for unqualified weapons to thrust. In shape-aware Heavy test at L20, pike primary F1.1745 yields only **~5.3% more same-A Power** than a greatsword thrust F1.1444125; versus END43/QII Boughsteel Medium armor, pike Impact≈1.80/stage2, greatsword thrust≈1.71/stage1. Wider tactical value and item-specific tuning still matter.
- **Armor:** Across 20 levels and four STR/SPD versus STR/END pairing cases with eligibility enforced, injury-stage reductions were **Light QI→QII: 8/80**, **Medium QI→QII: 18/72**, **Heavy QI→QII: 8/20**, **Light QI→Medium QI: 44/72**, **Medium QI→Heavy QI: 18/20**. Category changes from material/class SPD penalties were also checked; armor class improvement is potent but can ease incoming hit difficulty. Counts are *deterministic scenarios, not hit odds*.
- **Investment:** At L16 both in QII Medium with Medium blunt reference, STR/SPD attacking STR/END is **Easy, 1 injury conditional**, while reverse is **Very Hard, 1 injury conditional**. In QI Light, reverse can deliver **3 injuries** if it actually connects. At L20 STR/DEX Both governing A≈57.3625 versus one-stat STR51.25, but SPD19 versus fast build SPD38; the stronger dual-stat attacks remain harder to land against the fast build.

**Judgment:** Keep approved global weapon-form, Junk, armor, attribute, Guard and hit-category rules unchanged. Potential **greatsword versatility versus pike dedicated-Piercing distinctiveness** deserves targeted individual-F/geometry checks; Quality/class armor investment and SPD/END provide real conditional tradeoffs. **Unresolved:** actual hit percentages, outcomes/durations, individual item Fs/minima, offhand/shield interference, extended materials/species, magic, costs and specific weapon eligibility. No new simulator-dice system or probability claim introduced.

**Files:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` holds full test method and tabulations; this changelog records exploratory status; `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` points to findings.

## November 12, 2026 — Fractional Junk accessory bonuses final-floor APPROVED; levels 1–20 two-track combat tests

**Latest explicit author approval:** For Junk QI accessories, their **halved numerical attribute bonuses** are allowed to contribute real fractional **+0.5 stat points**; **add all separately legal equipped accessory contributions affecting the SAME stat before rounding the final affected stat DOWN ONCE**. Example: two +0.5 accessories give +1; three +0.5 accessories give +1.5, so an otherwise integer stat20 becomes21. Do NOT floor each individual +0.5 early, round every item up, pool bonuses between unrelated stats, create half sockets, or invent extra Soul Strain reductions. Original Centaur half-bonus and item-level racial rounding remain a **specific exception to preserve**; full mixed-affinity rounding ordering for multiple accessory groups is a future edge-case check. No general global change to unrelated fractional racial effective attributes was approved by this Junk accessories-only discussion. **Actual enchant-slot capacities, odd-number and nonstat enhancement mechanics remain open.**

**Combat test user request fulfilled:** Run two levels **1 through 20** sets: **A normal-equipped enemies** (ordinary QI weapon and armor from first level, QII weapon at level16) and **B mirrored-upgrade enemies** (both start Junk and upgrade when Hero does). Test Hero chosen replacement phases **L1–5 Junk weapon+armor**, **L6–10 normal QI weapon/Junk armor**, **L11–15 normal QI weapon+armor**, **L16–20 normal QII weapon/QI armor**. Full equipment and purchase schedules are *testing choices*, not compulsory progression. Each of **20 levels × 2 tracks × 2 Hero stat allocations × 4 enemy SPD/END profiles = 320 deterministic encounter states** was checked. Hero exemplar is a canon Thornguard with a **working F1.15 Medium mace-like Bludgeoning Narrow** attack and QI Boughsteel Full Light AR25; melee individual-weapon F remains draft and QII raw output is held at same reference F to avoid inventing a new Power coefficient. No probability/dice, hit-rate or actual guaranteed victories are claimed: existing six-category attack ladder does not have numeric hit probabilities.

**Peer matchup results, Hero-outgoing/incoming difficulty:** L1–5 normal-enemy **Hard/Easy** versus mirror **Average/Average**; L6–10 normal **Average/Easy** versus mirror **Easy/Easy**; L11–20 both **Average/Average**. Thus early Junk **versus Junk cancels**, normal foes punish un-upgraded player, and simultaneous weapon upgrades with both still in Junk armor make both parties easier to hit for five levels. One fast foe (SPD×1.30) at level1 with normal equipment gives **Very Hard/Certain**, against mirrored Junk **Hard/Easy**. Extreme speedy foe (SPD×1.60) versus starting Junk player is **Impossible/Certain** when normally equipped, but **Very Hard/Easy** if also in Junk. Difficult combat must allow existing terrain, pressure, alternate damage and withdrawal; these are synthetic high-threat examples, not auto-win/lose dice predictions.

**Impact/Guard sensitivity:** Working F1.15 STR-led blunt strike, 25% physical AR bypass, QI Boughsteel Full Light AR25, and the latest post-rebase quadratic Power/END²+AR² yield fresh equal-gear Strength/Speed investment **Solid hit stage1 at levels1–7**, **Devastating stage2 at8–15**, **Overwhelming stage3 at16–20** when armor stays QI Light. With STR/END investment instead, matching level1–20 peers take only **Solid** initial landed hits under this exact example. Heroes retain five Shimmer Guard injury advancements and ordinary foes have three; a one-landed Overwhelming wound from around level16 can defeat the ordinary foe but cannot incapacitate a fresh Hero without separately authorized exceptions. **This observation is stat-allocation and frozen-low-quality armor dependent**, not reason to change already approved physical armor F/AR, global Junk modifiers or natural END. An additional level1 END×1.25 and SPD×0.75 bruiser produces a Player Impact Ratio **~0.69 Superficial** while the return is **~0.89 Solid**: in a hypothetical all-hit attrition stress sequence the Hero cannot wound this opponent before losing. No actual stochastic combat win rate is possible from current canon.

**Result/recommendations:** Retain approved Junk penalties unchanged. The cost/accuracy tradeoffs are promising, particularly the starter-to-normal upgrade. **Monitor the vulnerable weapon-first / Junk-armor interval**, and in later test compare light armor QI→QII or better, and tactical alternative to inappropriate high-END brute-force encounters. Formal accuracy-percentage statistics and actual durations are not approved. **Tests and limits are documented in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`; no new tactical probabilities or enemy templates are canon.** `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` already owns the six-category implementation; no new combat rules were added.

**Files updated in this approval/test round:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. Starting Junk grants, gem/Glint budget, conditional 50% shop buyback, 30%-of-lifetime Gem consumables, material-neutral pricing and Celestite prohibitions remain unchanged.

## November 12, 2026 — Junk equipment handling penalty, half enchant capacity and starter gear approved; first tests

**Latest user explicit approval (supersedes older "Junk drawback numerics unresolved" records):** The already-approved Junk condition costs **50%** of the comparable ordinary **Quality I, Boughsteel-based** same-form equipment. A Junk weapon's **attacks are one hit-difficulty CATEGORY HARDER for the wielder to land**; Junk armor makes **ordinary evasion-resolved attacks against its wearer one category EASIER for the attacker to land**. These are shifts on the existing Certain/Easy/Average/Hard/Very Hard/Impossible six-category ladder; apply each relevant Junk role once per attack, so Junk weapon versus Junk armor nets zero; do not stack multiple worn Junk subpieces for repeated penalties. Keep normal categorical endpoints and all genuine attack-validity requirements. **Do not reduce weapon F/Power, armor AR/MR, physical STR/DEX/SPD/END, cause new automatic breakage, add a new percentage evasion system or grant extra attack actions.** The new rule is concrete even if the exact hit-probability algorithm remains intentionally narrative.

**Accessories and future magical implements:** A Junk accessory has **50% of ordinary Quality I bonuses AND 50% ordinary QI enchantment potential**. Future Junk implements should follow the same principle, but **implement mechanics are intentionally not designed yet**. Rounding half of odd-number numerical effects, fractional socket/charge capacity and non-numeric properties are unresolved; no invented half socket, new Soul Strain discount, free magic or forced stat floor. Junk shields and special blocking interactions still await normal shield-form adjudication.

**New starting-character rule APPROVED:** A starting Hero receives **one eligible Junk weapon and one Junk armor ensemble** as **starting equipment**, tailored to anatomy and ordinary physical usability. They are granted items, **NOT extra cash, Gem Shop purchased merchandise or free 50% resale eligibility**. **Explicit author correction:** starter item value **may exceed W(1)=30 Gems**, which is a reference planning target, NOT a hard cap for initial gifted items. No mandatory balancing payout to other Heroes, no forced deduced Gem wallet, and no change to W10=200/W25=400 and later tier wealth targets. The free starter junk pair's appraisal does not imply the Hero has enough liquid Gems to immediately replace it.

**Small focused tier tests, not new catalogue tariffs:** Assume *illustrative category* standard QI primary 60/armor 40 Gems, Junk 30/20 and all other Junk-support positions 75. At **L1**, given 30+20=**50 Gems** of starting gear, 20 above W1, which is deliberate. At **L10** W200, keeping full Junk pair+support costs **125 Gems**, upgrading just weapon to normal QI → **155**, upgrading just armor to normal QI → **145**, spending 120 on normal QII weapon while retaining 95 in Junk support → **215 Gems**, exceeding W10 by 15 (omit support). At **L25** W400, normal QII weapon plus ordinary QI armor and Junk other support → **235 Gems**; proposed normal QIII primary OR substantively enchanted QII primary 240, Junk armor and Junk other support → **335 Gems**, preserving 65 of budget headroom but poor support gear. These are **total owned-portfolio planning values, not liquid cash**, and magic/enchant effects of Junk accessories cannot be assumed freely available. These results support gradual upgrading versus early primary specialization but do not test real shopping stock, exact magical affixes or character build output.

**Categorical combat tests:** An otherwise Average direct attack becomes **Hard** if made by Junk weapon only, **Easy** if target wears Junk armor only, and returns to **Average** if both apply. From Very Hard, Junk weapon may push to **Impossible**, a balance concern to watch in real play. No armor Defense/physical injury rates or actual probabilistic hit frequencies were altered or simulated.

**Updated owning references:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (operative Junk rules, starting items, arithmetic tests and limitations); `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` (combat-category interaction); this changelog and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` (approval and supersession). **Outstanding:** accessory half-bonus rounding and magical installation potential, junk shields and unusual forms, exact starting gear form/stocks, real encounter probabilities and equipment-price validation, and later implements. Retired material/price rules stay retired.

## November 12, 2026 — Material-neutral Hero Gems APPROVED; Celestite +20% price retired; impurity purely narrative; focused tests

**Latest explicit author approval, superseding our prior open question:** At matching **equipment design/weapon shape or armor class, coverage, workmanship Quality, and comparable enchantment installation**, **chosen material does not add or subtract any standard Hero Gem-equivalent cost**. Characters specializing in STR/DEX/other stats should not face a second material rarity or power premium just because they invested to meet item requirements. **Material still determines all previously approved Power/AR/MR, weight/handling, mobility costs, vulnerabilities, attunement, special abilities and real physical construction restrictions.** This applies to weapons, armor and other otherwise equivalent forms; don't exempt a special-material device through a disguised cost surcharge. Separately unusual engineered design or independently installed magic can carry their own price. No economic rule silently changes existing mechanics.

**Celestite author-approved price correction:** The immediately previous **1.20× equivalent Adamantite QV armor crafting valuation is NOW SUPERSEDED** (older ×5 historical already retired). New nominal **Celestite QV = ×1.00 comparable Adamantite QV** at same equipment design/class/coverage/workmanship. This is a *Gem-equivalent appraisal*, not a shop sale: **Celestite still QV ONLY**, requiring real material, extraordinary qualified Centaur smith and **simultaneous personal aid of Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet**, and still imposes approved intrinsic MP drain, Leydrain, Soul Shred/XP erosion. No buyer can substitute Gems for divine help or obtain an ordinary vendor-stock Celestite item. Older Celestite-specific material price table and prior 20% record remain as **SUPERSEDED** historical records.

**Author clarification: purity is ONLY an IN-WORLD explanation:** No material purity stat, refinement level, alloy percentage gate, extra item-tier/Quality, bonus penalty to F/AR/MR/handling, or impurity progression is introduced. A future **parallel conventional-currency economy** will separately value bar purity, real quantities and labor; Centaurs can already **craft and enchant without touching divine Gems**. The author's bar example is illustrative: one nearly pure Adamantite bar might provide material for many lightly-alloyed swords, whereas a massive greatsword might consume multiple bars. These are **NOT approved recipes, mass ratios or mundane coin valuations**, and no Gem-to-mortal-currency exchange is declared. All current standard Gem-price references are game-economy planning, not proof that ores are equally expensive in mortal markets.

**Focused tests documented in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`:** At STR40 QV Heavy STR-led reference Boughsteel Power ~3639 versus Adamantite ~5685 with the matching one-time ×1.25 material F, **+56.25%** physical Power, while illustrative QV Heavy maul stat minima **STR16 vs STR38**. QV Full Heavy Boughsteel **AR115/STR36/SPD−20%**, Adamantite **AR170/STR91/SPD−30%**, Nullporcelain **AR0/MR170/STR26/SPD−15%**, Glassweave **AR115/MR115/STR35/SPD−19%** but shatter vulnerability; Bellcopper **AR115/STR48/SPD−25%** has only conditional ready ×2 defense; Celestite **AR180/MR185/STR65/SPD−24%** requires triune forging and imposes intrinsic **15 MP/turn** plus **5% of first-donning XP snapshot per voluntarily engaged combat turn**. These preserve strong niche identities and attribute access but **do not prove global game balance**. Adamantite can dominate a qualified weapon comparison absent other nonprice tradeoffs; Bellcopper and Glassweave are situational priorities to test with actual impacts, repairs and spell threats. No weapon/armor stats or penalties were altered.

**Scope and files:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` now owns the pricing rule and test report; `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md` records independent mundane forging and the Celestite exception; `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` records NO combat changes; `10_Canon_Changelog.md` and `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` record canon precedence. **W100=3,200 Gems**, 30%-primary-item pattern, 100 Glints/Gem, 30% of lifetime Gems spent on consumables, conditional Gem Shop 50% buyback, Quality I Boughsteel-only Junk 50% cost, and enchanted item ~×2 price guideline remain unchanged. Exact base class/coverage/form prices, Junk mechanical weakness, specialty crafted forms, enchanting installation price/strain, actual mundane economy, and Celestite sourcing remain unresolved.

## November 12, 2026 — Enchanted Primary Item ×2 and Junk gear accepted; equal-material pricing considered

**Explicit author approval:** Accept the latest proposed early equipment purchasing strategy, with the author's correction that a tier one step above costs **60% of typical portfolio**, but **two steps above 120%**, NOT 60%. Keep normal tier I–V budget milestones **200/400/800/1600/3200 Gems** and typical unenchanted Primary Item targets **60/120/240/480/960 Gems**. The 30% Primary Item is the Hero's single largest deliberate investment, weapon for many or armor/shield for others, **not a surcharge** based on declaration. There is no new use-level requirement.

**Author-approved economic guideline:** A **meaningfully enchanted** item of the same actual Quality, form and functional type should cost **around ×2** the corresponding ordinary nonmagical item; typical primary investments become **120/240/480/960/1920 Gems** when fully enchanted. This is NOT a new physical Quality VI or unrestricted enchant access; Soul Strain, race exceptions, rune and gem eligibility remain as established, with magic effect-specific fees to be developed. Existing **3040-Gem full-slot example** had mixed form/magic cost allocations, so it cannot be simultaneously charged a second full enchantment fee without recomputing; switching its 960-Gem primary to a 1920-Gem one costs **4000 total**, which exceeds W100 by 800 Gems and requires supporting compromises. Tiny utility runes need not double every item automatically.

**Author-approved Junk category:** All Junk equipment is **Quality I, Boughsteel-based**, and costs **half the corresponding standard Quality I same-shape/category item price**. For example, normal QI primary **60 Gems** versus Junk **30 Gems**. Junk is not a new sixth Quality, and has intentionally inferior condition/reliability to make standard QI a dependable upgrade. **Exact combat penalties, repair/breakage, item eligibility, nonmetal accessory implementation and magical compatibility remain unresolved**; not silently equal to QI performance for half cost. At level10 W200, QI primary + ten half-price QI support examples = **155 Gems**, while a QII primary + ten Junk support is **215 Gems**, forcing at least 15 Gems in supporting tradeoffs.

**Equal-cost material policy question currently UNDER REVIEW:** The author suggests that two items of the same design, coverage and Quality should have the same standard Gem cost across materials, because the character already pays attribute/handling tradeoffs (e.g. Adamantite stronger but harder to wield). They further **suggest** removing Celestite's existing +20% Gem valuation for consistency. This is not yet an explicit final price-rule approval: **retain the approved Celestite Quality V crafting valuation ×1.20 comparable Adamantite** until the author confirms equal-price material treatment. Existing material factors, mandatory deity/Smithcraft Celestite production and non-shop availability remain intact. `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` owns the approved shopping/junk guidelines and open material proposal.

**Files changed:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. No other Gem prices, combat factors or divine material prerequisites changed in this update.

## November 12, 2026 — Gem economy rebased ×10 from 320-Gem test to W100=3,200; Glints and full equipment repricing

**Latest explicit author instruction and scope:** Accept the *last proposed* **320-Gem level-100** equipment model as the new economic anchor and **multiply all Gem values in its tier and 11-slot example tables by ×10**. Author further directs a broad revisiting of gear prices to fit a psychologically robust economy: one **Primary Item** represents roughly **30%** of a Hero's completed equipment portfolio, and a normal item one tier up costs about **double** and therefore consumes ~60% of current-tier wealth. Primary is **one prioritized item** (weapon for most, armor or shield for others), **not** a price modifier applied arbitrarily to any object.

**New AUTHOR-DIRECTED WORKING Wealth values (SUPERSEDING OLD W100=4000 CURVE):** Starter level1 current-equipment target **30 Gems**; reference I/II/III/IV/V at level 10/25/50/75/100 **200/400/800/1600/3200 Gems**. Primary-item price targets **60/120/240/480/960 Gems**. Old 4,000-Gem W100, the 3+3997((L−1)/99)^2.3 curve, previous budgeted 7,872 lifetime Gems, and earlier failed 400-Gem QV maul/480–500-Gem Greatbow proposals are historical and **NOT current recommendations**. `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` owns a piecewise-interpolated W_new(L) tied to the six level1–100 milestones.

**Indivisible whole-Gem source and smaller denomination:** To permit low per-encounter/monster rewards while keeping whole-Gem units, introduce **100 Glints = 1 Gem** as the current small currency accounting convention. Exact fractional Gem economic balances can be represented by whole integer Glints, with sub-Glint theoretical award remainders deferred/aggregated rather than distributing fractions. This does not add free mana/SP/stat points, change divine quest qualification, or invent a mortal-world exchange rate.

**Lifetime economics still preserve 30% consumables and qualifying 50% Gem Shop buyback:** Reference tier portfolio lost value cumulatively 0/100/300/700/1500 Gems; level100 earned `(3200+1500)/.70≈6714.29` Gems; consumption `≈2014.29` Gems; final equipment `3200`. Without qualifying resale and otherwise same full portfolio-replacement scenario, gross life earnings to maintain the same final equipment would need `(3200+3000)/.7≈8857.14` Gems; this is sensitivity, not a new automatic payout. Retain actual source-dependent eligibility.

**Author-requested last 11-item example chart ×10, still price targets rather than approved enchantment tariffs:** One 6 QV / 5 QIII full equipment illustration is 11 slots: primary **960**, armor **640**, offhand/shield **320**, head **60**, neck **240**, arms **60**, hands **60**, feet **70**, waist **240**, ringA **320**, ringB **70** = **3040 Gems**, **160 Gems unallocated** against 3200. Illustrative equipment Soul Strain remains **50** on a normally MP100 Hero; the monetary ×10 does not multiply Soul Strain, attributes, AR, F, movement, Quality physical effects or skill/spell costs. Different race innate MP eligibility remains.

**Named approved vendor/utility Gem price redenomination:** The original Moonlit Garden starter goods and active Shimmer conveniences (including inventory/cartography), ordinary 40-arrow mixed refill and Cogsong Command Looms have **×10 nominal new Gem prices** to retain original purchasing-power proportions. For example, starter Willow Shortbow including 12 arrows **20 new Gems**, Hand Crossbow including 12 bolts **30 new Gems**, 40 replacement ordinary arrows **10 new Gems**, formerly `12/28/55/90/140` Looms **120/280/550/900/1400** Gems; all effects, counts, class eligibility and Craft ranks unchanged. Surrender's secret 50–250-Gem original reward reference becomes **500–2500 current Gems**. Retired Moonlit Forge +1..+5 independent reinforcement is still retired; original historical quoted costs do not become active upgrade fees.

**Broad equipment REPRICING PASSES, clearly NOT new final material prices:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` includes (1) all eleven slot category quality I–V planning bands, (2) a specific 3040-Gem level100 mixed-tier example, (3) proposed standardized ordinary full-coverage armor QV Light/Medium/Heavy comparisons across the eleven ordinary materials plus Celestite (QV only), (4) six physical composite QV prices, (5) physical weapon baseline **640-Gem QV Boughsteel Heavy melee core** and proposed QV engineered bow physical prices **320/480/640/800 Gems**, Greatbow +25% over the physical 640-Gem comparator. All these NEW numerical category/material/crafting/bow prices are **UNAPPROVED cost suggestions** requiring author review and production/effect balance, **not** replacement canon vendor prices merely by being archived on `main`.

**Pricing exploit found:** High-force bow draw cap and its Power are from engine construction, not mainly from Quality. Generic Quality prices halve by tier; blindly multiplying a Quality I STR400-engineered Greatbow to 800/16=50 Gems permits a qualified very-high-STR user to buy legendary draw capability for starter money. Reserve an independent mandatory **engineering cost floor** for high-capacity bows and any intrinsic-special-effect gear and calibrate Quality workmanship surcharge separately. This is price architecture testing only, no invented bow power, ammo size rating, rare material forced recipe, DEX, SP or skill penalty.

**Outstanding:** Real enchantment costs vs Soul Strain; actual smith material/labor values; legitimate market valuations for 11 armor materials and composites; quality-limited exceptional weapon engineering floor; remaining ammo beyond ordinary arrows; mundane currency/Glint handling specifics; finished item and shield/weapon cost distribution; approved Gem Shop price versus bespoke craft provenance; true campaign wallet migration of old balance entries; many pre-existing old economic numbers preserved in explicitly **HISTORICAL** text should not be interpreted as current. Economic budgets are author-directed working targets, *not* automatic player rewards or guaranteed physical feasibility.

**Files updated in this round:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (owning revised economy, shop tables, comparative price models), `01_World_and_Shimmer.md` (new headline/small denomination, Shimmer upgrades and secret reward amount), `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md` (Loom redenomination, structural cost-proposal separation), `27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md` and `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` (arrow price conversion), `10_Canon_Changelog.md` (this record), `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` (current navigation). Historical campaign transaction files are not retroactively rewritten.

A **NEW NOT-YET-APPROVED distribution timing remedy** spreads each upcoming expected 50%-resale replacement-loss reserve across the preceding 15/25-level interval; preserves the exact tier totals and W100=3200/E100≈6714.29 while reducing 99→100 new budget to **~137.14 Gems**. This is a **reward allocation proposal**, not an extra Gem grant or a change to 50% shop sale eligibility. Another scenario may include level1→level10 starter replacement: loss **15 Gems** on 30-Gem starter items if actually qualified and fully sold, raising modeled gross to **~6735.71 Gems**; not included in the selected 1500-loss example. ## November 12, 2026 — Celestite crafting markup reduced to 20%; heavy-bow prices under review

**Celestite armor author-approved revision:** The earlier **5× nominal same-class/same-coverage Quality V Adamantite armor price** is **SUPERSEDED**. New approved Celestite exceptional *crafting-purpose* valuation is **1.20× comparable Adamantite armor value** (a 20% premium), while Celestite remains the extraordinary top-priced material benchmark. It is craftable by the appropriately qualified Centaur smith with real Celestite, divine assistance and the established **simultaneous cooperation of Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet**. Ordinary sale is not available; the price isn't a purchase shortcut. Since no actual Adamantite armor Gem price has yet been approved, the formula is approved but no exact Celestite Gem number is. No AR/MR/STR/SPD/Leydrain/Soul Shred/MP/XP or magic price changed. The earlier noncanon Full Heavy sample **1,050 Gem Adamantite ×5 = 5,250 Gem Celestite** is historical and now **1,050 ×1.20 = 1,260 Gems** in *hypothetical math only*, not a new armor price.

**Heavy bow price feedback, NOT numeric approval of replacements:** The author challenges the currently provisional **150/450/950/1,750 Gem QV bow targets**, especially the 1,750 Greatbow, because ordinary two-handed STR-led melee weapons already naturally scale physical Power with STR. Author proposes considering **just 20%–25% extra relative to the finished normal Heavy weapon baseline** for the heaviest engineered bow category, while specifically demanding conflict/balance review **before changing bow prices**. Therefore preserve four existing working prices as **flagged for revision**, keep approved bow Power/range/handling/ordinary ammo untouched, and **do not activate a 1.20–1.25 universal bow markup** until properly matched ordinary Heavy two-handed melee weapon pricing and author explicit acceptance. Potential countervailing value is ranged engagement, ordinary primary Piercing 50% AR bypass, and tactical positioning; possible melee advantages include unlimited personal-STR scaling, damage via form/material/Quality, weapon reach/coverage and no ammo. These support a modest engineering surcharge philosophically, not a required gigantic cost difference. Current basic QV 90 Gem is a generic Quality benchmark, not the normal heavy weapon baseline.

**Scope, unresolved and files:** Update `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md`, `27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`, `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. Historical contrary 5× Celestite references are marked superseded, not silently erased.

## November 12, 2026 — 11 equipped slots adopted for reverse-engineered endgame economy

**Latest explicit author direction:** Proceed with the formerly **proposed slot philosophy as though it were canon** during the complete-loadout pricing pass. The current **approved working economic equipment layout** is **11 positions**: one armor ensemble, one head, one neck, one arms/bracers, one hands/gloves, one feet/boots, one waist, two actively enchanted ring positions, primary weapon/implement, and offhand weapon/shield/second implement. This supersedes leaving slot count unresolved for current level-100 pricing exercises. Do not revive the older optional independently layered Light underarmor, stack physical AR from separate slot accessories, ignore two-handed weapon constraints, or confuse these with six free Shimmer inventory storage slots. Detailed definition in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`; previous slot discussion in `24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md` flagged as adopted only in this narrow extent.

**Reverse engineering performed, NOT approval of the new prices:** Three deliberately backward-fit **6x Quality V / 5x Quality III** examples were constructed, all within the existing 4,000-Gem level-100 portfolio and using approximately half natural MP as equipment Strain (assuming ordinarily mana-generating natural MP equal to effective END). **Heavy guardian: 2,305 Gem physical + 1,610 Gem magic = 3,915 Gem, 50 strain / 100 MP, 85 Gem spare. Legendary greatbow marksman: 2,705 Gem physical + 975 Gem magic = 3,680 Gem, 40 strain / 80 MP, 320 spare. Spellcaster: 1,495 Gem physical + 2,415 Gem magic = 3,910 Gem, 60 strain / 120 MP, 90 spare.** All form/enchantment slot prices in those examples are **NONCANON targets**. The Greatbow's existing 1,750 Gem provisional **mundane finished** price is respected, plus a *hypothetical* separate 120-Gem magical installation. The guardian's hypothetical **1,050-Gem QV Full Heavy Adamantite unenchanted armor** is NOT approved armor price; 5x=5,250 Gems would be merely a hypothetical Celestite extraordinary craft valuation at that price before independent enchants, not a canon price.

**Interpretation:** Demonstrates mathematical *possibility* for 4,000-Gem richly equipped Heroes, **not proof current equipment economy is synchronized**. Two sample builds have only 85–90 Gem room, and higher authentic enchantment or Adamantite item costs could break them. Upward/downward adjustment to current wealth is deferred until independently grounded equipment and enchantment price calibrations. Preserve 30% lifetime Gem earnings consumed by consumables, reference full replacement and provenance-limited 50% Gem Shop buyback, the separate exceptional Celestite crafting requirements, and the armor-first pause on ammunition economics.

**Files changed:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`, `24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`. **Unresolved:** authorized installed-slot effects, enchantment surcharge formulas, full armor/Adamantite price ladder, racial strain exceptions, realistic loadouts and possible future wealth-curve adjustment.

## November 12, 2026 — Level-100 full-loadout economy goal and Celestite crafting-value clarification (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Latest user benchmark (approved design objective; new numbers not yet selected):** Level-100 Hero wealth must be calibrated to purchase **armor, primary weapon, a legitimate offhand weapon/shield, and approximately one piece in each eventually defined gear slot**, with **about half of the equipped items top-of-line and half midgame-grade**. For a race/build with meaningful natural mana, the array should support **roughly half the natural MP capacity invested in actual equipment Soul Strain**; preserve race-specific MP/strain differences and separate magic from physical Quality. This target supersedes developing isolated armor prices without checking the *total* endgame build budget. It is **not** final approval of any specific slot list, final item price, complete enchantment formula, or a fixed universal level-100 END.

**Accounting audit:** Approved W(100)=**4,000 Gem current portfolio**, 30% of lifetime Gem *earnings* to consumables, about 7,872 lifetime reference earnings / 1,510.5 reference resale losses. Existing finished-weapon QV Greatbow price target **1,750 Gem (43.75% of W100)** is a provisional expensive specialist reference. Base Quality I–V **14/30/50/70/90 Gem** values are not universally completed item prices. Example NONCANON slot scenario: 11 items with six QV/five QIII imply quality anchors **790 Gems**; replacing one QV 90-Gem placeholder with Greatbow 1,750 gives **2,450 Gems**, only **1,550 Gems** left for material/form/enchantment premiums across ten others. If all eleven example items were magically enhanced with their QV/QIII standard 8/4 strain, the hypothetical **68 equipment strain** would equal half natural MP only at MP136. This highlights a real **missing-calibration / possible affordability mismatch**, but does not demonstrate that the 4,000 Gem income curve is numerically wrong because finished armor, shield, secondary-weapon and other enchantment prices and gear slots are not settled. Do not add 30% consumables atop the current 4,000 equipment budget, or assume every mundane quality item causes full reference Soul Strain.

**Critical missing dependencies:** Approved equipped-slot inventory and how it interacts with full armor, accessory enchantment prices and cumulative strain, normal/rare material class- and quality-dependent armor/weapon/offhand finished costs, representative END/MP builds, special crafting/availability. **Plan next**: make one or more explicit illustrative complete level-100 equipment inventories and solve per-slot/enchantment budgets backward before committing Adamantite prices. Any trial table must be labeled PROPOSED and preserve baseline income until evidence supports revision.

**Celestite author clarification:** Its existing **5× comparable Adamantite Quality V armor valuation is a crafting-purpose benchmark**, although no final Adamantite numeric Gem price exists. Celestite is **craftable by a qualifying Smith with required divine cooperation** (under canon: Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet, advanced Centaur smithing and scarce material), **not ordinarily purchasable**. Calling it 'nonpurchasable display price' should not imply it is solely decorative or cannot be created. No conventional crafting shortcut/MP/SP/price is invented and Soul Shred/Leydrain persist.

**Affected:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (owning economy and Celestite clarification), `07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md` (extraordinary craft clarification), `10_Canon_Changelog.md` (this update), `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md` (scope/status). ## November 11, 2026 — Ordinary arrow supply approved; ammo pricing sequenced after armor price baseline

The five standard **14/30/50/70/90 Gem** Quality benchmarks are general equipment reference values, **not** material- and armor-class-specific prices; **4,000 Gems** is a reference level-100 equipment portfolio, not the price of an Adamantite suit. Celestite's nonpurchasable **5× nominal Adamantite armor display valuation** depends on the as-yet-unpriced underlying armor item. The **40 arrows / 1 Gem** bundle is accepted working canon, but **do not use it to extrapolate prices for quivers, crossbow bolts, firearm cartridges or special arrows until armor-first equipment economy calibration**.
